::: {#scatter .section}
### [Scatter](#id94){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#scatter "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[Scatter]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/scatter.html#Scatter){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Scatter "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Plot-local scatter style settings.

    This class controls the style of drawn scatter points on a specific
    plot.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import *

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        infile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'HeatExchanger.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(infile)

        frame = tp.active_frame()
        frame.plot_type = PlotType.Cartesian2D
        plot = frame.plot()
        plot.contour(0).variable = dataset.variable('T(K)')
        plot.show_scatter = True

        plot.scatter.variable = dataset.variable('P(N)')

        for z in dataset.zones():
            scatter = plot.fieldmap(z).scatter
            scatter.symbol_type = SymbolType.Geometry
            scatter.symbol().shape = GeomShape.Circle
            scatter.fill_mode = FillMode.UseSpecificColor
            scatter.fill_color = plot.contour(0)
            scatter.color = plot.contour(0)
            scatter.size_by_variable = True

        frame.add_text('Size of dots indicate relative pressure', (20, 80))

        # ensure consistent output between interactive (connected) and batch
        plot.contour(0).levels.reset_to_nice()

        tp.export.save_png('scatter.png')
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/scatter.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img

    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.layout.html#tecplot.layout.Frame "tecplot.layout.Frame"){.reference
    .internal} that holds this [[`ContourGroup`{.xref .any .py .py-class

    .internal}. Example usage:

[[Scatter.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[variable_index]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Scatter.variable_index "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Zero-based index of the [[`Variable`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.AuxDataObjectType.Variable "tecplot.constant.AuxDataObjectType.Variable"){.reference
    .internal} used for size of scatter symbols.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.scatter.variable_index = dataset.variable('P').index
        >>> plot.fieldmap(0).scatter.size_by_variable = True
    :::
    ::::

    The [[`Dataset`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.data.html#tecplot.data.Dataset "tecplot.data.Dataset"){.reference
    .internal} attached to this contour group's [[`Frame`{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.layout.html#tecplot.layout.Frame "tecplot.layout.Frame"){.reference
    .internal} is used, and the variable itself can be obtained through
    it:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> scatter = plot.scatter
        >>> scatter_var = dataset.variable(scatter.variable_index)
        >>> scatter_var.index == scatter.variable_index
        True
    :::
    ::::
:::

::: {#scatterreferencesymbol .section}
### [ScatterReferenceSymbol](#id95){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#scatterreferencesymbol "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[ScatterReferenceSymbol]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[scatter]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/symbol.html#ScatterReferenceSymbol){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Reference symbol for scatter plots.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    The reference scatter symbol is only shown when the scatter symbols
    are sized by a [[`Variable`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.AuxDataObjectType.Variable "tecplot.constant.AuxDataObjectType.Variable"){.reference
    .internal} in the [[`Dataset`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.data.html#tecplot.data.Dataset "tecplot.data.Dataset"){.reference
    .internal}.
    :::

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import *

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        infile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'HeatExchanger.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(infile)

        frame = tp.active_frame()
        frame.plot_type = PlotType.Cartesian2D
        plot = frame.plot()
        plot.contour(0).variable = dataset.variable('T(K)')
        plot.show_scatter = True

        plot.scatter.variable = dataset.variable('P(N)')

        plot.scatter.reference_symbol.show = True
        plot.scatter.reference_symbol.symbol().shape = GeomShape.Circle
        plot.scatter.reference_symbol.magnitude = plot.scatter.variable.max()
        plot.scatter.reference_symbol.color = Color.Green
        plot.scatter.reference_symbol.fill_color = Color.Green
        plot.scatter.reference_symbol.position = (20, 81)

        frame.add_text('Size of dots indicate relative pressure', (23, 80))

        for z in dataset.zones():
            scatter = plot.fieldmap(z).scatter
            scatter.symbol_type = SymbolType.Geometry
            scatter.symbol().shape = GeomShape.Circle
            scatter.fill_mode = FillMode.UseSpecificColor
            scatter.fill_color = plot.contour(0)
            scatter.color = plot.contour(0)
            scatter.size_by_variable = True

        # ensure consistent output between interactive (connected) and batch
        plot.contour(0).levels.reset_to_nice()

        tp.export.save_png('scatter_reference_symbol.png')
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/scatter_reference_symbol.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/scatter_reference_symbol.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/scatter_reference_symbol.png" /></a>
    </figure>

      [[`fill_color`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.fill_color "tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.fill_color"){.reference .internal}               The fill [[`Color`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Color "tecplot.constant.Color"){.reference .internal} of the reference symbol.
      [[`filled`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.filled "tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.filled"){.reference .internal}                           Fill the background area behind the reference symbol.
      [[`line_thickness`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.line_thickness "tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.line_thickness"){.reference .internal}   Edge line thickness for geometry reference symbols.
      [[`magnitude`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.magnitude "tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.magnitude"){.reference .internal}                  Symbol size relative to data variable ranges.
      [[`position`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.position "tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.position"){.reference .internal}                     The [\\((x, y)\\)]{.math .notranslate .nohighlight} position of the reference symbol.
      [[`show`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.show "tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.show"){.reference .internal}                                 Display a reference scatter symbol on the plot.
      [[`symbol_type`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.symbol_type "tecplot.plot.ScatterReferenceSymbol.symbol_type"){.reference .internal}            The type of symbol to display.

### [Vector2D](#id96){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#vector2d "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[Vector2D]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/vector.html#Vector2D){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Vector2D "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Vector field style control for Cartesian 2D plots.

    This object controls the style of the vectors that are plotted
    according to the vector properties under fieldmaps. The
    [\\((u,v)\\)]{.math .notranslate .nohighlight} components are set
    using this class as well as attributes such as length, arrow-head
    size and the reference vector. This example shows how to show the
    vector field, adjusting the arrows color and thickness:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import *

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        infile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', '3ElementWing.lpk')
        tp.load_layout(infile)

        frame = tp.active_frame()
        dataset = frame.dataset
        plot = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian2D)

        frame.background_color = Color.Black
        for axis in plot.axes:
            axis.show = False

        plot.axes.x_axis.min = -0.2
        plot.axes.x_axis.max = 0.3
        plot.axes.y_axis.min = -0.2
        plot.axes.y_axis.max = 0.15

        vect = plot.vector
        vect.u_variable = dataset.variable('U(M/S)')
        vect.v_variable = dataset.variable('V(M/S)')
        vect.relative_length = 0.00025
        vect.size_arrowhead_by_fraction = False
        vect.arrowhead_size = 4
        vect.arrowhead_angle = 10

        plot.show_contour = False
        plot.show_streamtraces = False
        plot.show_edge = True
        plot.show_vector = True

        cont = plot.contour(0)
        cont.variable = dataset.variable('P(N/M2)')
        cont.colormap_name = 'Diverging - Blue/Yellow/Red'
        cont.levels.reset_levels(80000, 90000, 100000, 110000, 120000)

        plot.fieldmaps().show = False

        fmap = plot.fieldmap(3)
        fmap.show = True
        fmap.edge.color = Color.White
        fmap.edge.line_thickness = 1
        fmap.points.step = 5
        fmap.vector.color = cont
        fmap.vector.line_thickness = 0.5

        tp.export.save_png('vector2d.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/vector2d.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/vector2d.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/vector2d.png" /></a>
    </figure>

[[Vector2D.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[even_spacing]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Vector2D.even_spacing "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Spacing for even vectors.

    Set the spacing values in axial directions for even spaced vectors.
    When [`use_even_spacing`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} is
    turned on this will selectively remove vectors so that only one
    vector occupies each grid region defined by the spacing. Spacing is
    aligned with the X and Y axes.

    Example of setting spacing in X to 0.1 and Y to 0.2:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.vector.use_even_spacing = True
        >>> plot.vector.even_spacing = (0.1, 0.2)
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

:   Magnitude-varying length of the vector line.

    When [`use_relative`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} is

    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference

        .external} (grid units or cm per magnitude)

:   Use even spacing for vectors.

    When [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} this will selectively remove vectors from the plot to
    approximately enforce the display intervals specified by
    [`even_spacing`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}.

    Turn on even spacing:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.vector.use_even_spacing = True
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[Vector2D.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[use_grid_units]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Vector2D.use_grid_units "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Use grid-units when determining the relative length.

    This takes effect only if [`use_relative`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre} is [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}. If [[`False`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#False "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}, [`relative_length`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre} will be in cm per magnitude:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.vector.use_relative = True
        >>> plot.vector.use_grid_units = False
        >>> plot.vector.relative_length = 0.010
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[Vector2D.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[use_relative]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Vector2D.use_relative "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Use relative sizing for vector lines.

    This determines whether [`length`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre} or [`relative_length`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre} are used to size the arrow lines. Example
    usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.vector.use_relative = False
        >>> plot.vector.relative_length = 0.5
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

    .internal} of the plotted vectors.

    Vectors are plotted as [\\((u,v,w)\\)]{.math .notranslate
    .nohighlight}. Example usage:

### [Vector3D](#id97){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#vector3d "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[Vector3D]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/vector.html#Vector3D){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Vector3D "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Vector field style control for Cartesian 3D plots.

    This object controls the style of the vectors that are plotted
    according to the vector properties under fieldmaps. The
    [\\((u,v,w)\\)]{.math .notranslate .nohighlight} components are set
    using this class as well as attributes such as length, arrow-head
    size and the reference vector. See the [[`example`{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}[`for`{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}[`2D`{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}[`vector`{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}[`plots`{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Vector2D "tecplot.plot.Vector2D"){.reference
    .internal}.

:   Size of the arrowhead when sizing by fraction.

    The [`size_arrowhead_by_fraction`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre} property must be set to [[`True`{.xref .any
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} for this to take effect:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.vector.size_arrowhead_by_fraction = True
        >>> plot.vector.arrowhead_fraction = 0.4
    :::
    ::::

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference

:   Size of arrowhead when sizing by frame height.

    The [`size_arrowhead_by_fraction`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre} property must be set to [[`False`{.xref .any
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#False "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}

[[Vector3D.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[even_spacing]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Vector3D.even_spacing "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Spacing for even vectors.

    Set the spacing values in axial directions for even spaced vectors.
    When [`use_even_spacing`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} is
    turned on this will selectively remove vectors so that only one
    vector occupies each grid region defined by the spacing. Spacing is
    aligned with the X, Y, and Z axes.

    Example of setting spacing in X to 0.1, Y to 0.2 and Z to 0.3:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.vector.use_even_spacing = True
        >>> plot.vector.even_spacing = (0.1, 0.2, 0.3)
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[Vector3D.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[length]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Vector3D.length "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Length of all vectors when not using relative sizing.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.vector.use_relative = False
        >>> plot.vector.length = 5
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external} (percent of plot height)

<!-- -->

[[Vector3D.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[reference_vector]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Vector3D.reference_vector "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Vector field reference vector.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.vector.reference_vector.show = True
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`ReferenceVector`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ReferenceVector "tecplot.plot.ReferenceVector"){.reference
        .internal}

<!-- -->

[[Vector3D.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[relative_length]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Vector3D.relative_length "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Magnitude-varying length of the vector line.

    When [`use_relative`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} is
    [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}, the length of the vectors will be relative to the
    magnitude of the velocity vector values in the data field, scaled by
    this parameter which is either grid-units or centimeters per unit
    magnitude depending on the value of [`use_grid_units`{.docutils
    .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.vector.use_relative = True
        >>> plot.vector.use_grid_units = True
        >>> plot.vector.relative_length = 0.003
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external} (grid units or cm per magnitude)

<!-- -->

::: {#referencevector .section}
### [ReferenceVector](#id98){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#referencevector "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[ReferenceVector]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[vector]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/vector.html#ReferenceVector){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.ReferenceVector "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Vector field reference vector.

    The reference vector is a single arrow with an optional label
    indicating the value of the shown reference length:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import *

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        infile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'VortexShedding.plt')
        tp.data.load_tecplot(infile)

        frame = tp.active_frame()
        dataset = frame.dataset
        plot = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian2D)

        for txt in frame.texts():
            frame.delete_text(txt)

        vector_contour = plot.contour(0)
        vector_contour.variable = dataset.variable('T(K)')
        vector_contour.colormap_name = 'Magma'
        vector_contour.colormap_filter.reversed = True
        vector_contour.legend.show = False
        base_contour = plot.contour(1)
        base_contour.variable = dataset.variable('P(N/M2)')
        base_contour.colormap_name = 'GrayScale'
        base_contour.colormap_filter.reversed = True
        base_contour.legend.show = False

        vector = plot.vector
        vector.u_variable = dataset.variable('U(M/S)')
        vector.v_variable = dataset.variable('V(M/S)')
        vector.relative_length = 1E-5
        vector.arrowhead_size = 0.2
        vector.arrowhead_angle = 16

        ref_vector = vector.reference_vector
        ref_vector.show = True
        ref_vector.position = 50, 95
        ref_vector.line_thickness = 0.4
        ref_vector.label.show = True
        ref_vector.label.format.format_type = NumberFormat.FixedFloat
        ref_vector.label.format.precision = 1
        ref_vector.magnitude = 100

        fmap = plot.fieldmap(0)
        fmap.contour.flood_contour_group = base_contour
        fmap.vector.color = vector_contour
        fmap.vector.line_thickness = 0.4

        plot.show_contour = True
        plot.show_streamtraces = False
        plot.show_vector = True

        plot.axes.y_axis.min = -0.005
        plot.axes.y_axis.max = 0.005
        plot.axes.x_axis.min = -0.002
        plot.axes.x_axis.max = 0.008

        tp.export.save_png('vector2d_reference.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/vector2d_reference.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/vector2d_reference.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/vector2d_reference.png" /></a>
    </figure>

::: {#contourlegend .section}
### [ContourLegend](#id100){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#contourlegend "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.legend.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[ContourLegend]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[contour]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[\*]{.pre}]{.o}[[svargs]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/legend/contour_legend.html#ContourLegend){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Contour legend attributes.

    This class allows you to customize the appearance of the contour
    legend. The contour legend can be positioned anywhere inside the
    frame using the [[`position`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.position "tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.position"){.reference
    .internal} attribute of this class. Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        import os
        import numpy as np

        import tecplot
        from tecplot.constant import *

        # By loading a layout many style and view properties are set up already
        examples_dir = tecplot.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = os.path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'RainierElevation.lay')
        tecplot.load_layout(datafile)

        frame = tecplot.active_frame()
        plot = frame.plot()

        # Rename the elevation variable
        frame.dataset.variable('E').name = "Elevation (m)"

        # Set the levels to nice values
        plot.contour(0).levels.reset_levels(np.linspace(200,4400,22))

        legend = plot.contour(0).legend
        legend.show = True
        legend.vertical = False  # Horizontal
        legend.auto_resize = False
        legend.label_step = 5

        legend.overlay_bar_grid = False
        legend.position = (55, 94)  # Frame percentages

        legend.box.box_type = TextBox.None_ # Remove Text box

        legend.header.font.typeface = 'Courier'
        legend.header.font.bold = True

        legend.number_font.typeface = 'Courier'
        legend.number_font.bold = True

        tecplot.export.save_png('legend_contour.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/legend_contour.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/legend_contour.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/legend_contour.png" /></a>
    </figure>

    **Attributes**

      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
      [[`anchor_alignment`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.anchor_alignment "tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.anchor_alignment"){.reference .internal}         Anchor location of the legend.
      [[`auto_resize`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.auto_resize "tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.auto_resize"){.reference .internal}                        Skip levels to create a reasonably sized legend.
      [[`box`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.box "tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.box"){.reference .internal}                                                Legend box attributes.
      [[`header`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.header "tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.header"){.reference .internal}                                       [[`LegendHeader`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LegendHeader "tecplot.legend.LegendHeader"){.reference .internal} associated with a [[`ContourLegend`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend "tecplot.legend.ContourLegend"){.reference .internal}.
      [[`label_format`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_format "tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_format"){.reference .internal}                     Number formatting for labels along the legend.

[[ContourLegend.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[label_increment]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_increment "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Spacing between labels along the contour legend.

    Labels will be placed on the contour variable range from min to max.
    The smaller the increment value the more legend labels will be
    created. If the [[`label_location`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_location "tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_location"){.reference
    .internal} is [[`ContLegendLabelLocation.Increment`{.xref .any .py
    .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ContLegendLabelLocation.Increment "tecplot.constant.ContLegendLabelLocation.Increment"){.reference
    .internal}, labels are incremented by this value. For example, a
    [[`label_increment`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_increment "tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_increment"){.reference
    .internal} value of .5 will show labels at .5, 1.0, 1.5, etc.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    This value is only used if [[`label_location`{.xref .any .py
    .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_location "tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_location"){.reference
    .internal} is set to [[`ContLegendLabelLocation.Increment`{.xref
    .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ContLegendLabelLocation.Increment "tecplot.constant.ContLegendLabelLocation.Increment"){.reference
    .internal}. Otherwise it is ignored.
    :::

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import PlotType, ContLegendLabelLocation
        >>> legend = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian3D).contour(0).legend
        >>> legend.label_location = ContLegendLabelLocation.Increment
        >>> legend.label_increment = .5
    :::
    ::::

    ::: {.admonition .seealso}
    See also

    [[`label_location`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_location "tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_location"){.reference
    .internal}
    :::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[ContourLegend.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[label_location]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_location "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Placement of labels on the legend.

    If you have selected [[`ColorMapDistribution.Continuous`{.xref .any
    .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ColorMapDistribution.Continuous "tecplot.constant.ColorMapDistribution.Continuous"){.reference
    .internal} for the contour colormap filter distribution, you have
    three options for placement of labels on the legend:

    - [[`ContLegendLabelLocation.ContourLevels`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
      .docutils .literal
      .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ContLegendLabelLocation.ContourLevels "tecplot.constant.ContLegendLabelLocation.ContourLevels"){.reference
      .internal} - This option places one label for each contour level.
      See Contour Levels and Color.

    - [[`ContLegendLabelLocation.Increment`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
      .docutils .literal
      .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ContLegendLabelLocation.Increment "tecplot.constant.ContLegendLabelLocation.Increment"){.reference
      .internal} - Set [[`label_increment`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
      .docutils .literal
      .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_increment "tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_increment"){.reference
      .internal} to the increment value.

    - [[`ContLegendLabelLocation.ColorMapDivisions`{.xref .any .py
      .py-attr .docutils .literal
      .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ContLegendLabelLocation.ColorMapDivisions "tecplot.constant.ContLegendLabelLocation.ColorMapDivisions"){.reference
      .internal} - Places one label for each control point on the color
      map.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import PlotType, ContLegendLabelLocation
        >>> legend = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian3D).contour(0).legend
        >>> legend.label_location = ContourLevelLabelLocation.Increment
        >>> legend.label_increment = .5
    :::
    ::::

    ::: {.admonition .seealso}
    See also

    [[`label_increment`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_increment "tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.label_increment"){.reference
    .internal}
    :::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`ContLegendLabelLocation`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils
        .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ContLegendLabelLocation "tecplot.constant.ContLegendLabelLocation"){.reference
        .internal}

[[ContourLegend.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[position]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.position "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Position as a percentage of frame width/height.

    The legend is automatically placed for you. You may specify the
    [\\((x,y)\\)]{.math .notranslate .nohighlight} position of the
    legend by setting this value, where [\\(x\\)]{.math .notranslate
    .nohighlight} is the percentage of frame width, and [\\(y\\)]{.math
    .notranslate .nohighlight} is a percentage of frame height.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import PlotType
        >>> legend = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian3D).contour(0).legend
        >>> legend.position = (.1, .3)
        >>> pos = legend.position
        >>> pos.x  # == position[0]
        .1
        >>> pos.y  # == position[1]
        .3
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[ContourLegend.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[vertical]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.legend.ContourLegend.vertical "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Orientation of the legend.

    When set to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}, the legend is vertical. When set to [[`False`{.xref .any
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#False "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}, the legend is horizontal.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import PlotType
        >>> legend = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian3D).contour(0).legend
        >>> legend.vertical = False  # Show horizontal legend
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

        .external}

### [LegendHeader](#id101){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#legendheader "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.legend.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[LegendHeader]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[legend]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[\*]{.pre}]{.o}[[svargs]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/legend/contour_legend.html#LegendHeader){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.legend.LegendHeader "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   **Attributes**

      -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- ---------------------------------------------------
      [[`custom_text`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.custom_text "tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.custom_text"){.reference .internal}               A string to be used in the contour legend header.
      [[`font`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.font "tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.font"){.reference .internal}                                    Font used to display the legend header.

      [[`text_type`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.text_type "tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.text_type"){.reference .internal}                     Typesetter for the contour legend header.
      [[`use_custom_text`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.use_custom_text "tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.use_custom_text"){.reference .internal}   Use custom text on the contour legend header.

<!-- -->

[[LegendHeader.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[custom_text]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.custom_text "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   A string to be used in the contour legend header.

    If the [[`use_custom_text`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.use_custom_text "tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.use_custom_text"){.reference
    .internal} property is set to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}, the legend header will contain this text instead of the
    name of the variable assigned to the contour group. The text may
    contain Dynamic Text and formatting tags (see User Manual).

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import PlotType
        >>> plot = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian3D)
        >>> header = plot.contour(0).legend.header
        >>> header.use_custom_text = True
        >>> header.custom_text = 'time: &(SOLUTIONTIME) <greek>ms</greek>'
    :::
    ::::

    See also: [[`LegendHeader.use_custom_text`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.use_custom_text "tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.use_custom_text"){.reference
    .internal}

    ::: versionadded
    [New in version 2021.2: ]{.versionmodified .added}The ability to
    specify custom contour legend header text requires Tecplot 360 2021
    R2 or later.
    :::

    ::: versionadded
    [New in version 1.4.2.]{.versionmodified .added}
    :::

    Type[:]{.colon}

[[LegendHeader.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[text_type]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.legend.LegendHeader.text_type "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Typesetter for the contour legend header.

    This determines how the header text for the contour legend is
    rendered. Options are [[`TextType.Regular`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.TextType.Regular "tecplot.constant.TextType.Regular"){.reference
    .internal} or [[`TextType.LaTeX`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.TextType.LaTeX "tecplot.constant.TextType.LaTeX"){.reference
    .internal}. When using [`LaTeX`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}, make sure to use Python raw strings so that
    literal back-slashes are preserved.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import PlotType, TextType
        >>> plot = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian3D)
        >>> header = plot.contour(0).legend.header
        >>> header.text_type = TextType.LaTeX
        >>> header.use_custom_text = True
        >>> header.custom_text = r'\LaTeX'
    :::
    ::::

    ::: versionadded
    [New in version 2022.1: ]{.versionmodified .added}The ability to use
    LaTeX for contour legend header text requires Tecplot 360 2022 R1 or
    later.
    :::

    ::: versionadded
    [New in version 1.5.0.]{.versionmodified .added}
    :::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`TextType`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.TextType "tecplot.constant.TextType"){.reference
        .internal}

    .internal} property instead of the variable name assigned to the
    contour group.

        >>> header.use_custom_text = True
        >>> header.custom_text = 'Solution time: &(SOLUTIONTIME) ms'

    .internal}

    ::: versionadded
    [New in version 2021.2: ]{.versionmodified .added}The ability to
    specify custom contour legend header text requires Tecplot 360 2021
    R2 or later.
    :::

::: {#linelegend .section}
### [LineLegend](#id102){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#linelegend "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.legend.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[LineLegend]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/legend/line_legend.html#LineLegend){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.legend.LineLegend "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Line plot legend attributes.

    The XY line legend shows the line and symbol attributes of XY
    mappings. In [[`XY`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`line`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`plots`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.plot.html#tecplot.plot.XYLinePlot "tecplot.plot.XYLinePlot"){.reference
    .internal}, this legend includes the bar chart information. The
    legend can be positioned anywhere within the line plot frame by
    setting the [[`position`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LineLegend.position "tecplot.legend.LineLegend.position"){.reference
    .internal} attribute. By default, all mappings are shown, but
    [Tecplot
    360](https://www.tecplot.com/products/tecplot-360){.reference
    .external} removes redundant entries. Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        import os

        import tecplot
        from tecplot.constant import *

        examples_dir = tecplot.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = os.path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'Rainfall.dat')
        dataset = tecplot.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        frame = tecplot.active_frame()
        plot = frame.plot()
        frame.plot_type = tecplot.constant.PlotType.XYLine

        for i in range(3):
            plot.linemap(i).show = True
            plot.linemap(i).line.line_thickness = .4

        y_axis = plot.axes.y_axis(0)
        y_axis.title.title_mode = AxisTitleMode.UseText
        y_axis.title.text = 'Rainfall (in)'
        y_axis.fit_range_to_nice()

        legend = plot.legend
        legend.show = True
        legend.box.box_type = TextBox.Filled
        legend.box.color = Color.Purple
        legend.box.fill_color = Color.LightGrey
        legend.box.line_thickness = .4
        legend.box.margin = 5

        legend.anchor_alignment = AnchorAlignment.MiddleRight
        legend.row_spacing = 1.5
        legend.show_text = True
        legend.font.typeface = 'Arial'
        legend.font.italic = True

        legend.text_color = Color.Black
        legend.position = (90, 88)

        tecplot.export.save_png('legend_line.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/legend_line.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/legend_line.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/legend_line.png" /></a>
    </figure>

      [[`box`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LineLegend.box "tecplot.legend.LineLegend.box"){.reference .internal}                                          Legend box attributes.
      [[`font`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LineLegend.font "tecplot.legend.LineLegend.font"){.reference .internal}                                       Legend font attributes.
      [[`position`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LineLegend.position "tecplot.legend.LineLegend.position"){.reference .internal}                           Position as a percentage of frame width/height.
      [[`row_spacing`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LineLegend.row_spacing "tecplot.legend.LineLegend.row_spacing"){.reference .internal}                  Spacing between rows in the legend.
      [[`show`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LineLegend.show "tecplot.legend.LineLegend.show"){.reference .internal}                                       Show or hide the legend.
      [[`show_text`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LineLegend.show_text "tecplot.legend.LineLegend.show_text"){.reference .internal}                        Show/hide mapping names in the legend.
      [[`text_color`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.LineLegend.text_color "tecplot.legend.LineLegend.text_color"){.reference .internal}                     Color of legend text.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- -------------------------------------------------

::: {#rgbcoloringlegend .section}
### [RGBColoringLegend](#id103){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#rgbcoloringlegend "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.legend.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[RGBColoringLegend]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[rgb_coloring]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/legend/rgb_coloring_legend.html#RGBColoringLegend){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Legend for RGB coloring (multivariate contour) plots.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    The RGB coloring legend will only show when an active fieldmap's
    contour is being flooded by RGB.
    :::

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        import os
        import numpy as np

        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import *

        def normalize_variable(dataset, varname, nsigma=2):
            '''
            Normalize a variable such that the specified number of standard deviations
            are within the range [0.5, 1] and the mean is transformed to 0.5. The
            new variable will append " normalized" to the original variable's name.
            '''
            with tp.session.suspend():
                newvarname = varname + ' normalized'
                dataset.add_variable(newvarname)
                data = np.concatenate([z.values(varname).as_numpy_array()
                                       for z in dataset.zones()])
                vmin = data.mean() - nsigma * data.std()
                vmax = data.mean() + nsigma * data.std()
                for z in dataset.zones():
                    arr = z.values(varname).as_numpy_array()
                    z.values(newvarname)[:] = (arr - vmin) / (vmax - vmin)


        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        infile = os.path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'HeatExchanger.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(infile)

        frame = tp.active_frame()
        plot = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian2D)
        plot.show_contour = True

        # Variables must be normalized relative to each other
        # to make effective use of RGB coloring.
        normalize_variable(dataset, 'T(K)')
        normalize_variable(dataset, 'P(N)')

        plot.rgb_coloring.mode = RGBMode.SpecifyGB

        # all three channel variables must be set even if
        # we are only contouring on two of them.
        plot.rgb_coloring.red_variable = dataset.variable(0)
        plot.rgb_coloring.green_variable = dataset.variable('P(N) normalized')
        plot.rgb_coloring.blue_variable = dataset.variable('T(K) normalized')

        plot.rgb_coloring.legend.show = True
        plot.rgb_coloring.legend.green_label = 'Pressure'
        plot.rgb_coloring.legend.blue_label = 'Temperature'

        plot.fieldmaps().contour.flood_contour_group = plot.rgb_coloring

        tp.export.save_png('rgb_coloring_legend.png')
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/rgb_coloring_legend.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/rgb_coloring_legend.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/rgb_coloring_legend.png" /></a>
    </figure>

    **Attributes**

      [[`blue_label`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.blue_label "tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.blue_label"){.reference .internal}                                                         Label to use for the blue channel.

      [[`font`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.font "tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.font"){.reference .internal}                                                                           Legend font attributes.
      [[`green_label`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.green_label "tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.green_label"){.reference .internal}                                                      Label to use for the green channel.
      [[`height`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.height "tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.height"){.reference .internal}                                                                     Size of RGB coloring legend
      [[`orientation`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.orientation "tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.orientation"){.reference .internal}                                                      Placement of the RGB channels on the legend.
      [[`position`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.position "tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.position"){.reference .internal}                                                               Position as a percentage of frame width/height.
      [[`red_label`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.red_label "tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.red_label"){.reference .internal}                                                            Label to use for the red channel.
      [[`show`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.show "tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.show"){.reference .internal}                                                                           Display the RGB coloring legend.
      [[`show_labels`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.show_labels "tecplot.legend.RGBColoringLegend.show_labels"){.reference .internal}                                                      Show the RGB channel labels.

::: {#scatterlegend .section}
### [ScatterLegend](#id104){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#scatterlegend "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.legend.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[ScatterLegend]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[scatter]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/legend/scatter_legend.html#ScatterLegend){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.legend.ScatterLegend "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Legend style for scatter plots.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import *

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        infile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'HeatExchanger.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(infile)

        frame = tp.active_frame()
        frame.plot_type = PlotType.Cartesian2D
        plot = frame.plot()
        plot.show_scatter = True

        # make space for the legend
        plot.axes.viewport.right = 70
        plot.axes.x_axis.min = 4
        plot.axes.x_axis.max = 7

        # assign some shape and color to each fieldmap
        for i, fmap in enumerate(plot.fieldmaps()):
            for zone in fmap.zones:
                zone.name = 'Zone {}'.format(i)
            fmap.scatter.symbol().shape = GeomShape(i % 7)
            fmap.scatter.fill_mode = FillMode.UseSpecificColor
            fmap.scatter.fill_color = Color(i % 7)

        plot.scatter.legend.show = True
        plot.scatter.legend.row_spacing = 0.95

        tp.export.save_png('scatter_legend.png')
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/scatter_legend.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/scatter_legend.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/scatter_legend.png" /></a>
    </figure>

      [[`box`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ScatterLegend.box "tecplot.legend.ScatterLegend.box"){.reference .internal}                                          Legend box attributes.
      [[`font`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ScatterLegend.font "tecplot.legend.ScatterLegend.font"){.reference .internal}                                       Legend font attributes.
      [[`position`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ScatterLegend.position "tecplot.legend.ScatterLegend.position"){.reference .internal}                           Position as a percentage of frame width/height.
      [[`row_spacing`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ScatterLegend.row_spacing "tecplot.legend.ScatterLegend.row_spacing"){.reference .internal}                  Spacing between rows in the legend.
      [[`show`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.legend.ScatterLegend.show "tecplot.legend.ScatterLegend.show"){.reference .internal}                                       Show or hide the legend.

### [ContourGroup](#id105){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#contourgroup "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[ContourGroup]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[index]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/contour.html#ContourGroup){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.ContourGroup "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Contouring of a variable using a colormap.

    This object controls the style for a specific contour group within a
    [[`Frame`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.layout.html#tecplot.layout.Frame "tecplot.layout.Frame"){.reference
    .internal}. Contour levels, colormap and contour lines are accessed
    through this class:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import *

        # load data
        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = path.join(examples_dir,'SimpleData','CircularContour.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(datafile)
        plot = dataset.frame.plot()
        plot.show_contour = True

        contour = plot.contour(0)
        contour.variable = dataset.variable('Mix')
        contour.colormap_name = 'Magma'

        # ensure consistent output between interactive (connected) and batch
        contour.levels.reset_to_nice()

        # save image to file
        tp.export.save_png('contour_magma.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/contour_magma.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/contour_magma.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/contour_magma.png" /></a>
    </figure>

    There are a fixed number of contour groups available for each plot.
    Others can be enabled and modified by specifying an index other than
    zero:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> contour3 = plot.contour(3)
        >>> contour3.variable = dataset.variable('U')
    :::
    ::::

    **Attributes**

::: {#contourcolorcutoff .section}
### [ContourColorCutoff](#id106){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#contourcolorcutoff "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[ContourColorCutoff]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[contour]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/contour.html#ContourColorCutoff){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.ContourColorCutoff "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Color-mapped value limits to display.

    This lets you specify a range within which contour flooding and
    multi-colored objects, such as scatter symbols, are displayed:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        import os
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import PlotType, SurfacesToPlot

        # load the data
        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = os.path.join(examples_dir,'SimpleData','Pyramid.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        # show boundary faces and contours
        plot = tp.active_frame().plot()
        surfaces = plot.fieldmap(0).surfaces
        surfaces.surfaces_to_plot = SurfacesToPlot.BoundaryFaces
        plot.show_contour = True

        # cutoff contour flooding outside min/max range
        cutoff = plot.contour(0).color_cutoff
        cutoff.include_min = True
        cutoff.min = 0.5
        cutoff.include_max = True
        cutoff.max = 1.0
        cutoff.inverted = True

        # ensure consistent output between interactive (connected) and batch
        plot.contour(0).levels.reset_to_nice()

        tp.export.save_png('contour_color_cutoff.png',600)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/contour_color_cutoff.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/contour_color_cutoff.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/contour_color_cutoff.png" /></a>
    </figure>

    **Attributes**

      ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- --------------------------------------------------
      [[`include_max`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ContourColorCutoff.include_max "tecplot.plot.ContourColorCutoff.include_max"){.reference .internal}   Use the maximum cutoff value.
      [[`include_min`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ContourColorCutoff.include_min "tecplot.plot.ContourColorCutoff.include_min"){.reference .internal}   Use the minimum cutoff value.
      [[`inverted`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ContourColorCutoff.inverted "tecplot.plot.ContourColorCutoff.inverted"){.reference .internal}            Cuts values outside the range instead of inside.
      [[`max`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ContourColorCutoff.max "tecplot.plot.ContourColorCutoff.max"){.reference .internal}                           The maximum cutoff value.
      [[`min`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ContourColorCutoff.min "tecplot.plot.ContourColorCutoff.min"){.reference .internal}                           The minimum cutoff value.
      ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- --------------------------------------------------

<!-- -->

[[ContourColorCutoff.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[max]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.ContourColorCutoff.max "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   The maximum cutoff value.

    The [`include_max`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} must be
    set to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.contour(0).color_cutoff.include_max = True
        >>> plot.contour(0).color_cutoff.max = None
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external} or [[`None`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#None "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[ContourColorCutoff.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[min]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.ContourColorCutoff.min "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   The minimum cutoff value.

    The [`include_min`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} must be
    set to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.contour(0).color_cutoff.include_min = True
        >>> plot.contour(0).color_cutoff.min = 3.14
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external} or [[`None`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#None "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}
:::

::: {#contourcolormapfilter .section}
### [ContourColormapFilter](#id107){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#contourcolormapfilter "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[ContourColormapFilter]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[contour]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/contour.html#ContourColormapFilter){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.ContourColormapFilter "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Controls how the colormap is rendered for a given contour.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import *

        # load the data
        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = path.join(examples_dir,'SimpleData','HeatExchanger.plt')
        ds = tp.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        # set plot type to 2D field plot
        frame = tp.active_frame()
        frame.plot_type = PlotType.Cartesian2D
        plot = frame.plot()

        # show boundary faces and contours
        surfaces = plot.fieldmap(0).surfaces
        surfaces.surfaces_to_plot = SurfacesToPlot.BoundaryFaces
        plot.show_contour = True

        # by default, contour 0 is the one that's shown,
        # set the contour's variable, colormap and number of levels
        contour = plot.contour(0)
        contour.variable = ds.variable('P(N)')

        # cycle through the colormap three times and reversed
        # show a faithful (non-approximate) continuous distribution
        contour_filter = contour.colormap_filter
        contour_filter.num_cycles = 3
        contour_filter.reversed = True
        contour_filter.fast_continuous_flood = False
        contour_filter.distribution = ColorMapDistribution.Continuous

        # ensure consistent output between interactive (connected) and batch
        contour.levels.reset_to_nice()

        # save image to file
        tp.export.save_png('contour_filtered.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/contour_filtered.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/contour_filtered.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/contour_filtered.png" /></a>
    </figure>

      [[`continuous_min`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ContourColormapFilter.continuous_min "tecplot.plot.ContourColormapFilter.continuous_min"){.reference .internal}                        Lower limit for continuous colormap flooding.
      [[`distribution`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ContourColormapFilter.distribution "tecplot.plot.ContourColormapFilter.distribution"){.reference .internal}                              Rendering style of the colormap.
      [[`fast_continuous_flood`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ContourColormapFilter.fast_continuous_flood "tecplot.plot.ContourColormapFilter.fast_continuous_flood"){.reference .internal}   Use a fast approximation to continuously flood the colormap.

[[ContourColormapFilter.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[continuous_max]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.ContourColormapFilter.continuous_max "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Upper limit for continuous colormap flooding.

    Example set the limits to the (min, max) of a variable in a specific
    zone:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import ColorMapDistribution
        >>> cmap_filter = plot.contour(0).colormap_filter
        >>> cmap_filter.distribution = ColorMapDistribution.Continuous
        >>> pressure = dataset.variable('Pressure').values('My Zone')
        >>> cmap_filter.continuous_min = pressure.min()
        >>> cmap_filter.continuous_max = pressure.max()
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

[[ContourColormapFilter.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[distribution]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.ContourColormapFilter.distribution "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Rendering style of the colormap.

    Possible values:

    [[`Banded`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ColorMapDistribution.Banded "tecplot.constant.ColorMapDistribution.Banded"){.reference .internal}

    :   A solid color is assigned for all values within the band between
        two levels.

    [[`Continuous`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ColorMapDistribution.Continuous "tecplot.constant.ColorMapDistribution.Continuous"){.reference .internal}

    :   The color distribution assigns linearly varying colors to all
        multi-colored objects or contour flooded regions.

    Example:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import ColorMapDistribution
        >>> cmap_filter = plot.contour(0).colormap_filter
        >>> cmap_filter.distribution = ColorMapDistribution.Banded
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`ColorMapDistribution`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils
        .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ColorMapDistribution "tecplot.constant.ColorMapDistribution"){.reference
        .internal}

<!-- -->

[[ContourColormapFilter.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[fast_continuous_flood]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.ContourColormapFilter.fast_continuous_flood "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Use a fast approximation to continuously flood the colormap.

    Causes each cell to be flooded using interpolation between the color
    values at each node. When the transition from a color at one node to
    another node crosses over the boundary between control points in the
    color spectrum, fast flooding may produce colors not in the
    spectrum. Setting this to [[`False`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#False "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} is slower, but more accurate:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> cmap_filter = plot.contour(0).colormap_filter
        >>> cmap_filter.fast_continuous_flood = True
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

::: {#contourcolormapoverride .section}
### [ContourColormapOverride](#id108){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#contourcolormapoverride "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[ContourColormapOverride]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[index]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[colormap_filter]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/contour.html#ContourColormapOverride){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.ContourColormapOverride "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Assigns contour bands to specific color.

    Specific contour bands can be assigned a unique basic color. This is
    useful for forcing a particular region to use blue, for example, to
    designate an area of water. You can define up to 16 color overrides:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import *

        # load the data
        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'HeatExchanger.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        # set plot type to 2D field plot
        frame = tp.active_frame()
        frame.plot_type = PlotType.Cartesian2D
        plot = frame.plot()

        # show boundary faces and contours
        surfaces = plot.fieldmap(0).surfaces
        surfaces.surfaces_to_plot = SurfacesToPlot.BoundaryFaces
        plot.show_contour = True

        # by default, contour 0 is the one that's shown,
        # set the contour's variable, colormap and number of levels
        contour = plot.contour(0)
        contour.variable = dataset.variable('T(K)')
        contour.colormap_name = 'Sequential - Yellow/Green/Blue'
        contour.levels.reset(9)

        # turn on colormap overrides for this contour
        contour_filter = contour.colormap_filter
        contour_filter.show_overrides = True

        # turn on override 0, coloring the first 4 levels red
        contour_override = contour_filter.override(0)
        contour_override.show = True
        contour_override.color = Color.Red
        contour_override.start_level = 7
        contour_override.end_level = 8

        # save image to file
        tp.export.save_png('contour_override.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/contour_override.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/contour_override.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/contour_override.png" /></a>
    </figure>

    **Attributes**

      -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- ------------------------------------------------------
      [[`color`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ContourColormapOverride.color "tecplot.plot.ContourColormapOverride.color"){.reference .internal}                     Color which will override the colormap.
      [[`end_level`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ContourColormapOverride.end_level "tecplot.plot.ContourColormapOverride.end_level"){.reference .internal}         Last level to override.
      [[`show`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ContourColormapOverride.show "tecplot.plot.ContourColormapOverride.show"){.reference .internal}                        Include this colormap override when filter is shown.
      [[`start_level`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.ContourColormapOverride.start_level "tecplot.plot.ContourColormapOverride.start_level"){.reference .internal}   First level to override.
      -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- ------------------------------------------------------
