### [SliceGroup](#id120){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#slicegroup "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[SliceGroup]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[index]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/slice.html#SliceGroup){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Change attributes associated with a specific slice group.

    Slices can include lighting effects, contours, meshes, and more. To
    customize these and other attributes of slices, use this object.

    This object controls the style for a specific slice group within a
    [[`Frame`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.layout.html#tecplot.layout.Frame "tecplot.layout.Frame"){.reference
    .internal}. Slice [[`contour`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.contour "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.contour"){.reference
    .internal}, vector, edge, effects, mesh, visibility and position
    information are accessed through this class:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import *

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'DuctFlow.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        plot = tp.active_frame().plot()
        plot.contour(0).variable = dataset.variable('U(M/S)')
        plot.show_edge = True
        plot.fieldmap(0).edge.edge_type = EdgeType.Creases

        vectors = plot.vector
        vectors.u_variable = dataset.variable('U(M/S)')
        vectors.v_variable = dataset.variable('V(M/S)')
        vectors.w_variable = dataset.variable('W(M/S)')

        plot.show_slices = True
        slice_0 = plot.slice(0)

        slice_0.contour.show = True
        slice_0.contour.contour_type = ContourType.Overlay  # AKA "Both lines and flood"

        slice_0.effects.use_translucency = True
        slice_0.effects.surface_translucency = 30

        # Show an arbitrary slice
        slice_0.orientation = SliceSurface.Arbitrary
        slice_0.arbitrary_normal = (1, .5, 0)

        slice_0.show_primary_slice = False
        slice_0.show_start_and_end_slices = True
        slice_0.start_position = (-.21, .05, .025)
        slice_0.end_position = (1.342, .95, .475)
        slice_0.show_intermediate_slices = True
        slice_0.num_intermediate_slices = 3

        slice_0.edge.show = True
        slice_0.edge.line_thickness = 0.4

        # ensure consistent output between interactive (connected) and batch
        plot.contour(0).levels.reset_to_nice()

        tp.export.save_png('slice_group.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/slice_group.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/slice_group.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/slice_group.png" /></a>
    </figure>

    Up to eight different slice groups can be set. Each slice group can
    use different slice planes or different ranges for the same slice
    plane.

    :::: {.doctest .highlight-default .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> slice_3 = plot.slice(3)
        >>> slice_3.contour.show = True
    :::
    ::::

    ::: {.admonition .seealso}
    See also

    [[`SliceGroupCollection`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection "tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection"){.reference
    .internal}
    :::

    **Attributes**

      [[`clip`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.clip "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.clip"){.reference .internal}                                                                  Orientation of data clipping for this slice group.

      [[`edge`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.edge "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.edge"){.reference .internal}                                                                  Edge attributes for this slice group.

      [[`mesh`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.mesh "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.mesh"){.reference .internal}                                                                  Mesh attributes for this slice group.
      [[`num_intermediate_slices`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.num_intermediate_slices "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.num_intermediate_slices"){.reference .internal}         Number of intermediate slicing planes.
      [[`obey_source_zone_blanking`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.obey_source_zone_blanking "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.obey_source_zone_blanking"){.reference .internal}   Obey source zone blanking.
      [[`orientation`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.orientation "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.orientation"){.reference .internal}                                             Select on which plane the slice is drawn.
      [[`origin`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.origin "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.origin"){.reference .internal}                                                            Origin of the slice.

      [[`show`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show"){.reference .internal}                                                                  Show slices for this slice group.
      [[`show_intermediate_slices`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show_intermediate_slices "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show_intermediate_slices"){.reference .internal}      Show intermediate slices.
      [[`show_primary_slice`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show_primary_slice "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show_primary_slice"){.reference .internal}                        [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference .external}; Include the primary slice (first slice placed) in the [[Plot]{.std .std-ref}](tecplot.plot.html#plot){.reference .internal}.
      [[`show_start_and_end_slices`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show_start_and_end_slices "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show_start_and_end_slices"){.reference .internal}   Include start and end slices.
      [[`slice_source`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.slice_source "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.slice_source"){.reference .internal}                                          [[`SliceSource`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSource "tecplot.constant.SliceSource"){.reference .internal}: [[Zones]{.std .std-ref}](tecplot.data.html#data-access){.reference .internal} to slice through.
      [[`start_position`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.start_position "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.start_position"){.reference .internal}                                    Position of the start slice.
      [[`surface_generation_method`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.surface_generation_method "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.surface_generation_method"){.reference .internal}   Determines how the surface is generated.
      [[`use_slice_clipping`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.use_slice_clipping "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.use_slice_clipping"){.reference .internal}                        Clip this slice by any intersecting slices.
      [[`vector`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.vector "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.vector"){.reference .internal}                                                            Vector attributes for this slice group.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[[SliceGroup.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[clip]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.clip "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Orientation of data clipping for this slice group.

    Clipping the field data, isosurfaces, streamtraces or other plot
    objects using slices requires setting the [`clip`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre} property of the slice as well as adding the
    slice group to the [`clip_planes`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre} property on the fieldmap effects. Only slice
    groups 0 to 5 are available for clipping in this framework:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import *
        >>> # setup slice and clipping orientation
        >>> slice = plot.slice(0)
        >>> slice.origin = (7, 0, 0)
        >>> slice.clip = ClipPlane.AbovePrimarySlice
        >>> # add slice group to the fieldmap clip planes
        >>> plot.fieldmap(0).effects.clip_planes = [slice]
        >>> # turn on and set value of isosurface
        >>> # and enforce slice clipping
        >>> isosurf = plot.isosurface(0)
        >>> isosurf.isosurface_values = 3
        >>> isosurf.use_slice_clipping = True
        >>> # turn on isosurfaces and slices
        >>> pt.show_isosurfaces = True
        >>> pt.show_slices = True
    :::
    ::::

    ::: {.admonition .warning}
    Warning

    **Slice clipping is only supported for X, Y and Z-planes.**

    Slice clipping has no effect for slices that have an orientation of
    [[`SliceSurface.Arbitrary`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.Arbitrary "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.Arbitrary"){.reference
    .internal}, [[`SliceSurface.IPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.IPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.IPlanes"){.reference
    .internal}, [[`SliceSurface.JPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.JPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.JPlanes"){.reference
    .internal} or , [[`SliceSurface.KPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.KPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.KPlanes"){.reference
    .internal}. That is, the orientation must be one of:
    [[`SliceSurface.XPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.XPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.XPlanes"){.reference
    .internal}, [[`SliceSurface.YPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.YPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.YPlanes"){.reference
    .internal} or [[`SliceSurface.ZPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.ZPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.ZPlanes"){.reference
    .internal}.
    :::

    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show_start_and_end_slices "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show_start_and_end_slices"){.reference

    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} to show the end slice. This will be a 3-[[`tuple`{.xref
    .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference

    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference

    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.orientation "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.orientation"){.reference
    .internal} is [`X,Y,Z`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} or
    zero-based [[`int`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} if [[`orientation`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal

[[SliceGroup.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[extract]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[mode]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[ExtractMode.SingleZone]{.pre}]{.default_value}*, *[[assign_strand_ids]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[True]{.pre}]{.default_value}*, *[[resulting_1d_zone_type]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[Resulting1DZoneType.IOrderedIfPossible]{.pre}]{.default_value}*, *[[transient_mode]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[TransientOperationMode.SingleSolutionTime]{.pre}]{.default_value}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/slice.html#SliceGroup.extract){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.extract "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Create new zones from this slice.

    Extracts the current slices represented by this group to the
    [[`Dataset`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.data.html#tecplot.data.Dataset "tecplot.data.Dataset"){.reference
    .internal} as one or more zones.

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   - **mode** ([[`ExtractMode`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils
          .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ExtractMode "tecplot.constant.ExtractMode"){.reference
          .internal}, optional) -- Determines how many zones are created
          upon extraction. Possible values are:
          [[`ExtractMode.SingleZone`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
          .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ExtractMode.SingleZone "tecplot.constant.ExtractMode.SingleZone"){.reference
          .internal} (default) and
          [[`ExtractMode.OneZonePerConnectedRegion`{.xref .any .py
          .py-attr .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ExtractMode.OneZonePerConnectedRegion "tecplot.constant.ExtractMode.OneZonePerConnectedRegion"){.reference
          .internal}.

        - **assign_strand_ids** ([[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}, optional) -- Automatically assign strand ID's to
          the created zones. (default: [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils
          .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external})

        - **resulting_1d_zone_type** ([[`Resulting1DZoneType`{.xref .any
          .py .py-class .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Resulting1DZoneType "tecplot.constant.Resulting1DZoneType"){.reference
          .internal}, optional) -- The type of zone to create when the
          result is one-dimensional. Possible values are:
          [[`Resulting1DZoneType.IOrderedIfPossible`{.xref .any .py
          .py-attr .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Resulting1DZoneType.IOrderedIfPossible "tecplot.constant.Resulting1DZoneType.IOrderedIfPossible"){.reference
          .internal} (default) and
          [[`Resulting1DZoneType.FELineSegment`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
          .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Resulting1DZoneType.FELineSegment "tecplot.constant.Resulting1DZoneType.FELineSegment"){.reference
          .internal}.

        - **transient_mode** ([[`TransientOperationMode`{.xref .any .py
          .py-class .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode "tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode"){.reference
          .internal}) -- Determines which solution times are used to
          extract slices when transient data is available in the
          dataset. Possible values are
          [[`TransientOperationMode.SingleSolutionTime`{.xref .any .py
          .py-attr .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode.SingleSolutionTime "tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode.SingleSolutionTime"){.reference
          .internal} (default) or
          [[`TransientOperationMode.AllSolutionTimes`{.xref .any .py
          .py-attr .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode.AllSolutionTimes "tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode.AllSolutionTimes"){.reference
          .internal}.

    Returns[:]{.colon}

    :   The extracted zone is returned if **mode** is
        [[`ExtractMode.SingleZone`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
        .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ExtractMode.SingleZone "tecplot.constant.ExtractMode.SingleZone"){.reference
        .internal} and **transient_mode** is
        [[`TransientOperationMode.SingleSolutionTime`{.xref .any .py
        .py-attr .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode.SingleSolutionTime "tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode.SingleSolutionTime"){.reference
        .internal}, otherwise a
        [generator](https://docs.python.org/3/reference/expressions.html#generator-expressions){.reference
        .external} of the extracted zones.

    ::: {.admonition .seealso}
    See also

    [[`tecplot.data.extract.extract_slice()`{.xref .any .py .py-func
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.data.html#tecplot.data.extract.extract_slice "tecplot.data.extract.extract_slice"){.reference
    .internal}
    :::

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> slice_zone = plot.slice(0).extract()
    :::
    ::::

[[SliceGroup.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[obey_source_zone_blanking]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.obey_source_zone_blanking "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Obey source zone blanking.

    When set to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}, slices are subject to any blanking used for the data.
    When set to [[`False`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#False "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}, slices are generated for blanked and unblanked regions.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.slice(0).obey_source_zone_blanking = True
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

[[SliceGroup.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[orientation]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.orientation "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Select on which plane the slice is drawn.

    You may also choose [[`SliceSurface.Arbitrary`{.xref .any .py
    .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.Arbitrary "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.Arbitrary"){.reference
    .internal} to place the slice on an arbitrary plane.

    To orient slices in an arbitrary direction, choose
    [[`SliceSurface.Arbitrary`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.Arbitrary "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.Arbitrary"){.reference
    .internal}. As with other slices, you may specify origin points for
    a primary slice and/or for start and end slices. Slices pass through
    the indicated origin point(s), so you can easily align the edge of a
    slice or group of slices along some other feature of the plot, such
    as an axis. If intermediate slices are activated, they are drawn
    equally spaced between the slices defined by the start and end
    origins.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.slice(0).orientation = SliceSurface.XPlanes
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`SliceSurface`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface"){.reference
        .internal}

<!-- -->

[[SliceGroup.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[origin]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.origin "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Origin of the slice.

    This will be a 3-[[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} of [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} if [[`orientation`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.orientation "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.orientation"){.reference
    .internal} is [`X,Y,Z`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} or
    zero-based [[`int`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} if [[`orientation`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.orientation "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.orientation"){.reference
    .internal} is [`I,J,K`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}. For
    arbitrary slice orientation, the origin can be any location. For
    axis orientations ([[`XPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.XPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.XPlanes"){.reference
    .internal}, [[`YPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.YPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.YPlanes"){.reference
    .internal}, etc.) two of the three components are not used.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> slice_0 = plot.slice(0)
        >>> slice_0.orientation = SliceSurface.IPlanes
        >>> slice_0.origin = (1, 0, 0)
        >>> dx = (1, 1, 1)
        >>> slice_0.origin += dx
        >>> slice_0.origin.i
        2
        >>> slice_0.origin.j
        1
        >>> slice_0.origin.k
        1

        >>> slice_0.orientation = SliceSurface.Arbitrary
        >>> slice_0.origin = (.5, .1, .1)
        >>> slice_0.origin += dx
        >>> slice_0.origin.x
        1.5
        >>> slice_0.origin.y
        .1
        >>> slice_0.origin.z
        .1
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external} or [[`int`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[SliceGroup.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[set_arbitrary_from_points]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[p1]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[p2]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[p3]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/slice.html#SliceGroup.set_arbitrary_from_points){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.set_arbitrary_from_points "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Set an arbitrary slice from 3 points.

    Set the normal and origin of an arbitrary slice using three points.
    The origin will be set to the 3rd point.

    The three points must not be coincident or collinear. The slice's
    origin is set to the third point and its normal is recalculated such
    that the cutting plane passes through all three points.

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   - **p1** -- 3-[[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external} of floats [`(x,`{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}[`y,`{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}[`z)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}

        - **p2** -- 3-[[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external} of floats [`(x,`{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}[`y,`{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}[`z)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}

        - **p3** -- 3-[[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external} of floats [`(x,`{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}[`y,`{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}[`z)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> slice0 = plot.slice(0)
        >>> slice0.set_arbitrary_from_points((0.0, 0.0, 0.0),
        ...                                  (0.1, 0.2, 0.3),
        ...                                  (0.1, 0.1, 0.1))
    :::
    ::::

<!-- -->

[[SliceGroup.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[slice_source]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.slice_source "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   [[`SliceSource`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSource "tecplot.constant.SliceSource"){.reference
    .internal}: [[Zones]{.std
    .std-ref}](tecplot.data.html#data-access){.reference .internal} to
    slice through.

    Choose to slice through volume [[Zones]{.std
    .std-ref}](tecplot.data.html#data-access){.reference .internal},
    surface [[Zones]{.std
    .std-ref}](tecplot.data.html#data-access){.reference .internal}, or
    the surfaces of volume [[Zones]{.std
    .std-ref}](tecplot.data.html#data-access){.reference .internal}.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.slice(0).slice_source = SliceSource.SurfaceZones
    :::
    ::::

    [[`SliceGroup.show_start_and_end_slices`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show_start_and_end_slices "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show_start_and_end_slices"){.reference
    .internal} must be [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} to show the start slice. This will be a 3-[[`tuple`{.xref
    .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} of [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} if [[`orientation`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.orientation "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.orientation"){.reference
    .internal} is [`X,Y,Z`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} or
    zero-based [[`int`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} if [[`orientation`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.orientation "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.orientation"){.reference
    .internal} is [`I,J,K`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}.

[[SliceGroup.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[surface_generation_method]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.surface_generation_method "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Determines how the surface is generated.

    May be one of:

    - 

      [[`SurfaceGenerationMethod.Auto`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.Auto "tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.Auto"){.reference .internal}:

      :   Selects one of the surface generation algorithms best suited
          for the zones participating in the slice generation. "All
          polygons" is used if one or more of the participating zones is
          polytope, otherwise "allow quads" is used.

    - 

      [[`SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllPolygons`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllPolygons "tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllPolygons"){.reference .internal}:

      :   Similar to the "All triangles" method except that all interior
          faces generated as a result of triangulation that are not part
          of the original mesh are eliminated. This preserves the
          original mesh of the source zones on the resulting slice.

    - 

      [[`SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllTriangles`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllTriangles "tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllTriangles"){.reference .internal}:

      :   An advanced algorithm that can handle complex saddle issues
          and guarantees that there will be no holes in the final
          surface. As the surface is composed entirely of triangles, it
          can be delivered more efficiently to the graphics hardware.

    - 

      [[`SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllowQuads`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllowQuads "tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllowQuads"){.reference .internal}:

      :   Produces quads or triangles, and the resulting surface more
          closely resembles the shape of the volume cells from the
          source zone. Since the quads are not arbitrarily divided into
          triangles, no biases are introduced, and the resulting surface
          may appear smoother. This method is preferred when the source
          zone is FE-Brick or IJK-Ordered and the surface is aligned
          with the source cells.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import SurfaceGenerationMethod
        >>> plot.slice(0).surface_generation_method = \
        ...     SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllowQuads
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`SurfaceGenerationMethod`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils
        .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod "tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod"){.reference
        .internal}

<!-- -->

[[SliceGroup.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[use_slice_clipping]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.use_slice_clipping "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

::: {#slicegroupcollection .section}
### [SliceGroupCollection](#id121){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#slicegroupcollection "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[SliceGroupCollection]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[\*]{.pre}]{.o}[[indices]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/slice.html#SliceGroupCollection){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Change attributes associated with multiple slices.

    Slices can include lighting effects, contours, meshes, and more. To
    customize these and other attributes of slices, use this object. It
    controls the style for specific slice groups within a
    [[`Frame`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.layout.html#tecplot.layout.Frame "tecplot.layout.Frame"){.reference
    .internal}. Slice [[`contour`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.contour "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.contour"){.reference
    .internal}, vector, edge, effects, mesh, visibility and position
    information are accessed through this class:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import *

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'DuctFlow.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        plot = tp.active_frame().plot()
        plot.contour(0).variable = dataset.variable('U(M/S)')

        plot.show_slices = True
        slices = plot.slices(0, 1, 2)
        slices.show = True

        slices.contour.show = True
        slices.contour.contour_type = ContourType.Overlay
        slices.effects.use_translucency = True
        slices.effects.surface_translucency = 70

        # Show arbitrary slices
        slices.orientation = SliceSurface.Arbitrary
        slices.origin = (0.1, 0.2, 0)
        slices[0].arbitrary_normal = (1, 0.5, 0)
        slices[1].arbitrary_normal = (0.2, 1, 0)
        slices[2].arbitrary_normal = (0, 0, 1)

        slices.edge.show = True
        slices.edge.line_thickness = 0.4

        plot.contour(0).levels.reset_to_nice()
        plot.contour(0).legend.show = False

        tp.export.save_png('slice_collection.png')
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/slice_collection.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/slice_collection.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/slice_collection.png" /></a>
    </figure>

    Up to eight different slice groups can be set and each slice group
    can use different slice planes or different ranges for the same
    slice plane.

    This class behaves like [[`SliceGroup`{.xref .any .py .py-class
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup"){.reference
    .internal} except that setting any underlying style will do so for
    all of the represented slice groups. The style properties are then
    always returned as a [[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} of properties, one for each group, ordered by index
    number. This means there is an asymmetry between setting and getting
    any property under this object, illustrated by the following
    example:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> slices = plot.slices(0, 1, 2)
        >>> slices.show = True
        >>> print(slices.show)
        (True, True, True)
    :::
    ::::

    All examples that set style on a single slice like the following:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.slice(0).contour.show = True
    :::
    ::::

    may be converted to setting the same style on all slice groups like
    so:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.slices().contour.show = True
    :::
    ::::

    ::: {.admonition .seealso}
    See also

    [[`SliceGroup`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup"){.reference
    .internal}
    :::

    ::: versionadded
    [New in version 1.2: ]{.versionmodified .added}Slice collection
    objects.
    :::

    **Attributes**

      [[`show`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.show "tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.show"){.reference .internal}                                                                  Show slices for this slice group.
      [[`show_intermediate_slices`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.show_intermediate_slices "tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.show_intermediate_slices"){.reference .internal}      Show intermediate slices.
      [[`show_primary_slice`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.show_primary_slice "tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.show_primary_slice"){.reference .internal}                        [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference .external}; Include the primary slice (first slice placed) in the [[Plot]{.std .std-ref}](tecplot.plot.html#plot){.reference .internal}.
      [[`show_start_and_end_slices`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.show_start_and_end_slices "tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.show_start_and_end_slices"){.reference .internal}   Include start and end slices.

[[SliceGroupCollection.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[clip]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.clip "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Orientation of data clipping for this slice group.

    Clipping the field data, isosurfaces, streamtraces or other plot
    objects using slices requires setting the [`clip`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre} property of the slice as well as adding the
    slice group to the [`clip_planes`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre} property on the fieldmap effects. Only slice
    groups 0 to 5 are available for clipping in this framework:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import *
        >>> # setup slice and clipping orientation
        >>> slice = plot.slice(0)
        >>> slice.origin = (7, 0, 0)
        >>> slice.clip = ClipPlane.AbovePrimarySlice
        >>> # add slice group to the fieldmap clip planes
        >>> plot.fieldmap(0).effects.clip_planes = [slice]
        >>> # turn on and set value of isosurface
        >>> # and enforce slice clipping
        >>> isosurf = plot.isosurface(0)
        >>> isosurf.isosurface_values = 3
        >>> isosurf.use_slice_clipping = True
        >>> # turn on isosurfaces and slices
        >>> pt.show_isosurfaces = True
        >>> pt.show_slices = True
    :::
    ::::

    ::: {.admonition .warning}
    Warning

    **Slice clipping is only supported for X, Y and Z-planes.**

    Slice clipping has no effect for slices that have an orientation of
    [[`SliceSurface.Arbitrary`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.Arbitrary "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.Arbitrary"){.reference
    .internal}, [[`SliceSurface.IPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.IPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.IPlanes"){.reference
    .internal}, [[`SliceSurface.JPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.JPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.JPlanes"){.reference
    .internal} or , [[`SliceSurface.KPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.KPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.KPlanes"){.reference
    .internal}. That is, the orientation must be one of:
    [[`SliceSurface.XPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.XPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.XPlanes"){.reference
    .internal}, [[`SliceSurface.YPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.YPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.YPlanes"){.reference
    .internal} or [[`SliceSurface.ZPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.ZPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.ZPlanes"){.reference
    .internal}.
    :::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`ClipPlane`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ClipPlane "tecplot.constant.ClipPlane"){.reference
        .internal}

[[SliceGroupCollection.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[end_position]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.end_position "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Position of the end slice.

    [[`SliceGroup.show_start_and_end_slices`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show_start_and_end_slices "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.show_start_and_end_slices"){.reference
    .internal} must be [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} to show the end slice. This will be a 3-[[`tuple`{.xref
    .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} of [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} if [[`orientation`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.orientation "tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.orientation"){.reference
    .internal} is [`X,Y,Z`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} or
    zero-based [[`int`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} if [[`orientation`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal

    .internal} is [`I,J,K`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}.

[[SliceGroupCollection.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[extract]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[mode]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[ExtractMode.SingleZone]{.pre}]{.default_value}*, *[[assign_strand_ids]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[True]{.pre}]{.default_value}*, *[[resulting_1d_zone_type]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[Resulting1DZoneType.IOrderedIfPossible]{.pre}]{.default_value}*, *[[transient_mode]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[TransientOperationMode.SingleSolutionTime]{.pre}]{.default_value}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/slice.html#SliceGroupCollection.extract){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.extract "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Create new zones from this slice collection.

    Extracts the current slices represented by this group to the
    [[`Dataset`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.data.html#tecplot.data.Dataset "tecplot.data.Dataset"){.reference
    .internal} as one or more zones.

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   - **mode** ([[`ExtractMode`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils
          .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ExtractMode "tecplot.constant.ExtractMode"){.reference
          .internal}, optional) -- Determines how many zones are created
          upon extraction. Possible values are:
          [[`ExtractMode.SingleZone`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
          .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ExtractMode.SingleZone "tecplot.constant.ExtractMode.SingleZone"){.reference
          .internal} (default) and
          [[`ExtractMode.OneZonePerConnectedRegion`{.xref .any .py
          .py-attr .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ExtractMode.OneZonePerConnectedRegion "tecplot.constant.ExtractMode.OneZonePerConnectedRegion"){.reference
          .internal}.

        - **assign_strand_ids** ([[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}, optional) -- Automatically assign strand ID's to
          the created zones. (default: [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils
          .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external})

        - **resulting_1d_zone_type** ([[`Resulting1DZoneType`{.xref .any
          .py .py-class .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Resulting1DZoneType "tecplot.constant.Resulting1DZoneType"){.reference
          .internal}, optional) -- The type of zone to create when the
          result is one-dimensional. Possible values are:
          [[`Resulting1DZoneType.IOrderedIfPossible`{.xref .any .py
          .py-attr .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Resulting1DZoneType.IOrderedIfPossible "tecplot.constant.Resulting1DZoneType.IOrderedIfPossible"){.reference
          .internal} (default) and
          [[`Resulting1DZoneType.FELineSegment`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
          .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Resulting1DZoneType.FELineSegment "tecplot.constant.Resulting1DZoneType.FELineSegment"){.reference
          .internal}.

        - **transient_mode** ([[`TransientOperationMode`{.xref .any .py
          .py-class .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode "tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode"){.reference
          .internal}) -- Determines which solution times are used to
          extract slices when transient data is available in the
          dataset. Possible values are
          [[`TransientOperationMode.SingleSolutionTime`{.xref .any .py
          .py-attr .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode.SingleSolutionTime "tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode.SingleSolutionTime"){.reference
          .internal} (default) or
          [[`TransientOperationMode.AllSolutionTimes`{.xref .any .py
          .py-attr .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode.AllSolutionTimes "tecplot.constant.TransientOperationMode.AllSolutionTimes"){.reference
          .internal}.

    Returns[:]{.colon}

    :   [generator](https://docs.python.org/3/reference/expressions.html#generator-expressions){.reference
        .external} of the extracted zones.

    ::: {.admonition .seealso}
    See also

    [[`tecplot.data.extract.extract_slice()`{.xref .any .py .py-func
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.data.html#tecplot.data.extract.extract_slice "tecplot.data.extract.extract_slice"){.reference
    .internal}
    :::

    Example usage showing how to convert the resulting generator into a
    reusable list object of zones:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> slice_zones = list(plot.slices(0, 1).extract())
    :::
    ::::

[[SliceGroupCollection.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[obey_source_zone_blanking]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.obey_source_zone_blanking "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Obey source zone blanking.

    When set to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}, slices are subject to any blanking used for the data.
    When set to [[`False`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#False "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}, slices are generated for blanked and unblanked regions.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.slice(0).obey_source_zone_blanking = True
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

[[SliceGroupCollection.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[orientation]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.orientation "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Select on which plane the slice is drawn.

    You may also choose [[`SliceSurface.Arbitrary`{.xref .any .py
    .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.Arbitrary "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.Arbitrary"){.reference
    .internal} to place the slice on an arbitrary plane.

    To orient slices in an arbitrary direction, choose
    [[`SliceSurface.Arbitrary`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.Arbitrary "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.Arbitrary"){.reference
    .internal}. As with other slices, you may specify origin points for
    a primary slice and/or for start and end slices. Slices pass through
    the indicated origin point(s), so you can easily align the edge of a
    slice or group of slices along some other feature of the plot, such
    as an axis. If intermediate slices are activated, they are drawn
    equally spaced between the slices defined by the start and end
    origins.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.slice(0).orientation = SliceSurface.XPlanes
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`SliceSurface`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface"){.reference
        .internal}

<!-- -->

[[SliceGroupCollection.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[origin]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.origin "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Origin of the slice.

    This will be a 3-[[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} of [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} if [[`orientation`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.orientation "tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.orientation"){.reference
    .internal} is [`X,Y,Z`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} or
    zero-based [[`int`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} if [[`orientation`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.orientation "tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.orientation"){.reference
    .internal} is [`I,J,K`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}. For
    arbitrary slice orientation, the origin can be any location. For
    axis orientations ([[`XPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.XPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.XPlanes"){.reference
    .internal}, [[`YPlanes`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.YPlanes "tecplot.constant.SliceSurface.YPlanes"){.reference
    .internal}, etc.) two of the three components are not used.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> slice_0 = plot.slice(0)
        >>> slice_0.orientation = SliceSurface.IPlanes
        >>> slice_0.origin = (1, 0, 0)
        >>> dx = (1, 1, 1)
        >>> slice_0.origin += dx
        >>> slice_0.origin.i
        2
        >>> slice_0.origin.j
        1
        >>> slice_0.origin.k
        1

        >>> slice_0.orientation = SliceSurface.Arbitrary
        >>> slice_0.origin = (.5, .1, .1)
        >>> slice_0.origin += dx
        >>> slice_0.origin.x
        1.5
        >>> slice_0.origin.y
        .1
        >>> slice_0.origin.z
        .1
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external} or [[`int`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

    .literal

    .internal} is [`X,Y,Z`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} or

    .literal

[[SliceGroupCollection.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[surface_generation_method]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.surface_generation_method "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Determines how the surface is generated.

    May be one of:

    - 

      [[`SurfaceGenerationMethod.Auto`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.Auto "tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.Auto"){.reference .internal}:

      :   Selects one of the surface generation algorithms best suited
          for the zones participating in the slice generation. "All
          polygons" is used if one or more of the participating zones is
          polytope, otherwise "allow quads" is used.

    - 

      [[`SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllPolygons`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllPolygons "tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllPolygons"){.reference .internal}:

      :   Similar to the "All triangles" method except that all interior
          faces generated as a result of triangulation that are not part
          of the original mesh are eliminated. This preserves the
          original mesh of the source zones on the resulting slice.

    - 

      [[`SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllTriangles`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllTriangles "tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllTriangles"){.reference .internal}:

      :   An advanced algorithm that can handle complex saddle issues
          and guarantees that there will be no holes in the final
          surface. As the surface is composed entirely of triangles, it
          can be delivered more efficiently to the graphics hardware.

    - 

      [[`SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllowQuads`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllowQuads "tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllowQuads"){.reference .internal}:

      :   Produces quads or triangles, and the resulting surface more
          closely resembles the shape of the volume cells from the
          source zone. Since the quads are not arbitrarily divided into
          triangles, no biases are introduced, and the resulting surface
          may appear smoother. This method is preferred when the source
          zone is FE-Brick or IJK-Ordered and the surface is aligned
          with the source cells.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import SurfaceGenerationMethod
        >>> plot.slice(0).surface_generation_method = \
        ...     SurfaceGenerationMethod.AllowQuads
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`SurfaceGenerationMethod`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils
        .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod "tecplot.constant.SurfaceGenerationMethod"){.reference
        .internal}

<!-- -->

[[SliceGroupCollection.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[use_slice_clipping]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroupCollection.use_slice_clipping "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Clip this slice by any intersecting slices.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import ClipPlane
        >>> slice = plot.slice(0)
        >>> slice.clip = ClipPlane.AbovePrimarySlice
        >>> plot.fieldmap(0).effects.clip_planes = slice
        >>> plot.slice(1).use_slice_clipping = True
    :::
    ::::

    ::: {.admonition .seealso}
    See also

    [[`SliceGroup.clip`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.clip "tecplot.plot.SliceGroup.clip"){.reference
    .internal}
    :::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference

::: {#slicecontour .section}
### [SliceContour](#id122){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#slicecontour "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[SliceContour]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[slice_group]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/slice.html#SliceContour){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceContour "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Contour attributes of the slice group.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import SliceSurface, ContourType

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'DuctFlow.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        plot = tp.active_frame().plot()

        plot.show_slices = True
        slice_0 = plot.slice(0)

        # Use contour(0) for Flooding and contour(2) for Lines
        plot.contour(0).variable = dataset.variable('P(N/m2)')
        plot.contour(2).variable = dataset.variable('T(K)')
        plot.contour(2).legend.show = False
        slice_0.contour.show = True
        slice_0.contour.flood_contour_group = plot.contour(0)
        slice_0.contour.line_contour_group = plot.contour(2)
        slice_0.contour.contour_type = ContourType.Overlay  # AKA "Both lines and flood"

        slice_0.show_primary_slice = False
        slice_0.show_start_and_end_slices = True
        slice_0.show_intermediate_slices = True
        slice_0.start_position = (-.21, .05, .025)
        slice_0.end_position = (1.342, .95, .475)
        slice_0.num_intermediate_slices = 3

        # ensure consistent output between interactive (connected) and batch
        slice_0.contour.flood_contour_group.levels.reset_to_nice()
        slice_0.contour.line_contour_group.levels.reset_to_nice()

        tp.export.save_png('slice_contour.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/slice_contour.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/slice_contour.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/slice_contour.png" /></a>
    </figure>

[[SliceContour.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[use_lighting_effect]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceContour.use_lighting_effect "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}; Enable lighting effect.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    Setting [[`SliceContour.use_lighting_effect`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceContour.use_lighting_effect "tecplot.plot.SliceContour.use_lighting_effect"){.reference
    .internal} will also set the same value for
    [[`SliceShade.use_lighting_effect`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceShade.use_lighting_effect "tecplot.plot.SliceShade.use_lighting_effect"){.reference
    .internal}, and vice-versa.
    :::

    The lighting effect is set with
    [[`SliceEffects.lighting_effect`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.lighting_effect "tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.lighting_effect"){.reference
    .internal}, and may be one of [[`LightingEffect.Gouraud`{.xref .any
    .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud"){.reference
    .internal} or [[`LightingEffect.Paneled`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Paneled "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Paneled"){.reference
    .internal}.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.show_slices = True
        >>> contour = plot.slice(0).contour
        >>> contour.use_lighting_effect = True
        >>> plot.slice(0).effects.lighting_effect = LightingEffect.Paneled
    :::
    ::::
:::

::: {#sliceedge .section}
### [SliceEdge](#id123){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#sliceedge "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[SliceEdge]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[slice_group]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/slice.html#SliceEdge){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceEdge "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Edge attributes of the slice group.

    When enabled, selected edge lines of all slices in this group will
    be shown:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'DuctFlow.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        plot = tp.active_frame().plot()
        plot.show_slices = True
        plot.contour(0).variable = dataset.variable('U(M/S)')

        slice_0 = plot.slice(0)
        slice_0.edge.show = True
        slice_0.edge.line_thickness = 0.8

        # ensure consistent output between interactive (connected) and batch
        plot.contour(0).levels.reset_to_nice()

        tp.export.save_png('slice_edge.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">

[[SliceEdge.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[edge_type]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceEdge.edge_type "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Edge type.

    There are two types of edges in [Tecplot
    360](https://www.tecplot.com/products/tecplot-360){.reference
    .external}: creases and borders.

    An edge border is the boundary of a [[Zone]{.std
    .std-ref}](tecplot.data.html#data-access){.reference .internal}. An
    edge crease appears when the inside angle between two cells is less
    than a user-defined limit. The inside angle can range from 0-180
    degrees (where 180 degrees indicates coplanar surfaces). The default
    inside angle for determining an edge crease is 135 degrees.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.slice(0).edge.show = True
        >>> plot.slice(0).edge.edge_type = EdgeType.BordersAndCreases
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`EdgeType`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.EdgeType "tecplot.constant.EdgeType"){.reference
        .internal}

<!-- -->

    :::

[[SliceEdge.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[show]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceEdge.show "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Show edges.

    This property must be set to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} to show any of the other edge properties.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.slice(0).edge.show = True
        >>> plot.slice(0).edge.edge_type = EdgeType.BordersAndCreases

::: {#sliceeffects .section}
### [SliceEffects](#id124){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#sliceeffects "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[SliceEffects]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[slice_group]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/slice.html#SliceEffects){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceEffects "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Slice effects for this slice.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'DuctFlow.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        plot = tp.active_frame().plot()

        plot.show_slices = True
        slice_0 = plot.slice(0)

        plot.contour(0).variable = dataset.variable('U(M/S)')
        slice_0.contour.show = True

        slice_0.effects.use_translucency = True
        slice_0.effects.surface_translucency = 70

        # ensure consistent output between interactive (connected) and batch
        plot.contour(0).levels.reset_to_nice()

        tp.export.save_png('slice_effects.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/slice_effects.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/slice_effects.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/slice_effects.png" /></a>
    </figure>

    **Attributes**

      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- ---------------------------------------------------
      [[`lighting_effect`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.lighting_effect "tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.lighting_effect"){.reference .internal}                  Surface lighting effect.
      [[`surface_translucency`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.surface_translucency "tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.surface_translucency"){.reference .internal}   Surface translucency of the slice group.
      [[`use_translucency`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.use_translucency "tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.use_translucency"){.reference .internal}               Enable surface translucency for this slice group.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- ---------------------------------------------------

<!-- -->

[[SliceEffects.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[lighting_effect]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.lighting_effect "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Surface lighting effect.

    Slice lighting effects must be enabled by setting
    [[`SliceContour.use_lighting_effect`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceContour.use_lighting_effect "tecplot.plot.SliceContour.use_lighting_effect"){.reference
    .internal} or [[`SliceShade.use_lighting_effect`{.xref .any .py
    .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceShade.use_lighting_effect "tecplot.plot.SliceShade.use_lighting_effect"){.reference
    .internal} to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} when setting this value.

    There are two types of lighting effects: Paneled and Gouraud:

    > <div>
    >
    > - 
    >
    >   [[`Paneled`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Paneled "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Paneled"){.reference .internal}: Within each cell, the color assigned to each area by
    >
    >   :   shading or contour flooding is tinted by a shade constant
    >       across the cell. This shade is based on the orientation of
    >       the cell relative to your 3D light source.
    >
    > - 
    >
    >   [[`Gouraud`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud"){.reference .internal}: This offers smoother, more continuous shading than
    >
    >   :   Paneled shading, but it also results in slower plotting and
    >       larger print files. [[`Gouraud`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    >       .docutils .literal
    >       .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud"){.reference
    >       .internal} shading is not continuous across zone boundaries
    >       unless face neighbors are specified in the data.
    >       [[`Gouraud`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    >       .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud"){.reference
    >       .internal} shading is not available for finite element
    >       volume [[Zones]{.std
    >       .std-ref}](tecplot.data.html#data-access){.reference
    >       .internal} when blanking is active. The zone's lighting
    >       effect reverts to [[`Paneled`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    >       .docutils .literal
    >       .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Paneled "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Paneled"){.reference
    >       .internal} shading in this case.
    >
    > </div>

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.slice(0).contour.use_lighting_effect = True
        >>> plot.slice(0).effects.lighting_effect = LightingEffect.Paneled
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`LightingEffect`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect"){.reference
        .internal}

<!-- -->

[[SliceEffects.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[surface_translucency]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.surface_translucency "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Surface translucency of the slice group.

    Slice surface translucency must be enabled by setting
    [[`SliceEffects.use_translucency`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.use_translucency "tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.use_translucency"){.reference
    .internal} = [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} when setting this value.

    Valid slice translucency values range from one (opaque) to 99
    (translucent).

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.slice(0).effects.use_translucency = True
        >>> plot.slice(0).effects.surface_translucency = 20
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`int`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[SliceEffects.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[use_translucency]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.use_translucency "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Enable surface translucency for this slice group.

    The surface translucency value can be changed by setting
    [[`SliceEffects.surface_translucency`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.surface_translucency "tecplot.plot.SliceEffects.surface_translucency"){.reference
    .internal}.

    Example usage:

    :   [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal

        .external}

        from os import path

::: {#sliceshade .section}
### [SliceShade](#id126){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#sliceshade "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[SliceShade]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[slice_group]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/slice.html#SliceShade){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.SliceShade "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Shade attributes of the slice group.

    Show shading on the slice when [[`SliceContour.show`{.xref .any .py
    .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceContour.show "tecplot.plot.SliceContour.show"){.reference
    .internal} has not been selected or is set to
    [[`ContourType.Lines`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.ContourType.Lines "tecplot.constant.ContourType.Lines"){.reference
    .internal}:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import Color

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'Pyramid.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        plot = tp.active_frame().plot()
        plot.show_slices = True
        plot.slice(0).contour.show = False
        shade = plot.slice(0).shade
        shade.show = True
        shade.color = Color.Red  # Slice will be colored solid red.
        tp.export.save_png('slice_shade.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
