[[StreamtraceRodRibbonEffects.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[lighting_effect]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonEffects.lighting_effect "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   

    Get/set the lighting algorithm used when lighting

    :   streamtrace rods and ribbons.

    Ribbon lighting effects must be enabled by setting
    [[`StreamtraceRodRibbonShade.use_lighting_effect`{.xref .any .py
    .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonShade.use_lighting_effect "tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonShade.use_lighting_effect"){.reference
    .internal} to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} when setting this value.

    Note that setting
    [[`StreamtraceRodRibbonShade.use_lighting_effect`{.xref .any .py
    .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonShade.use_lighting_effect "tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonShade.use_lighting_effect"){.reference
    .internal} will also set this value for [[`ribbon`{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal .notranslate}[`contours`{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonContour "tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonContour"){.reference
    .internal}.

    There are two types of lighting effects: Paneled and Gouraud:

    > <div>
    >
    > - 
    >
    >   [[`Paneled`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Paneled "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Paneled"){.reference .internal}: Within each cell, the color assigned to each area by
    >
    >   :   shading or contour flooding is tinted by a shade constant
    >       across the cell. This shade is based on the orientation of
    >       the cell relative to your 3D light source.
    >
    > - 
    >
    >   [[`Gouraud`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud"){.reference .internal}: This offers smoother, more continuous shading than
    >
    >   :   Paneled shading, but it also results in slower plotting and
    >       larger print files. [[`Gouraud`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    >       .docutils .literal
    >       .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud"){.reference
    >       .internal} shading is not continuous across zone boundaries
    >       unless face neighbors are specified in the data.
    >       [[`Gouraud`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    >       .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Gouraud"){.reference
    >       .internal} shading is not available for finite element
    >       volume [[Zone]{.std
    >       .std-ref}](tecplot.data.html#data-access){.reference
    >       .internal} when blanking is active. The zone's lighting
    >       effect reverts to [[`Paneled`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    >       .docutils .literal
    >       .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Paneled "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect.Paneled"){.reference
    >       .internal} shading in this case.
    >
    > </div>

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.streamtraces.rod_ribbon.shade.use_lighting_effect = True
        >>> plot.streamtraces.rod_ribbon.effects.lighting_effect = LightingEffect.Paneled
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`LightingEffect`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.LightingEffect "tecplot.constant.LightingEffect"){.reference
        .internal}

<!-- -->

[[StreamtraceRodRibbonEffects.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[surface_translucency]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonEffects.surface_translucency "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Surface translucency of the streamtraces ribbon.

    Surface translucency must be enabled by setting
    [[`StreamtraceRodRibbonEffects.use_translucency`{.xref .any .py
    .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonEffects.use_translucency "tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonEffects.use_translucency"){.reference
    .internal} = [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} when setting this value.

    Valid translucency values range from one (opaque) to 99
    (translucent).

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}

        >>> plot.streamtraces.rod_ribbon.effects.use_translucency = True
        >>> plot.streamtraces.rod_ribbon.effects.surface_translucency = 20
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`int`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[StreamtraceRodRibbonEffects.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[use_translucency]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonEffects.use_translucency "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Enable surface translucency.

    The surface translucency value can be changed by setting

    .internal}.

::: {#streamtracerodribbonmesh .section}
### [StreamtraceRodRibbonMesh](#id133){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#streamtracerodribbonmesh "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[StreamtraceRodRibbonMesh]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[robribbon]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/streamtrace.html#StreamtraceRodRibbonMesh){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonMesh "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Streamtraces rod/ribbon mesh attributes.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    To set the mesh color or line thickness, see
    [[`Streamtraces.color`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Streamtraces.color "tecplot.plot.Streamtraces.color"){.reference
    .internal} and [[`Streamtraces.line_thickness`{.xref .any .py
    .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Streamtraces.line_thickness "tecplot.plot.Streamtraces.line_thickness"){.reference
    .internal}.
    :::

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        import os
        import tecplot
        from tecplot.constant import *

        examples_dir = tecplot.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = os.path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'DownDraft.plt')
        dataset = tecplot.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        frame = tecplot.active_frame()
        frame.plot_type = tecplot.constant.PlotType.Cartesian3D

        plot = frame.plot()
        plot.fieldmap(0).surfaces.surfaces_to_plot = SurfacesToPlot.BoundaryFaces
        plot.show_mesh = False
        plot.show_shade = False
        plot.show_edge = True

        plot.vector.u_variable_index = 4
        plot.vector.v_variable_index = 5
        plot.vector.w_variable_index = 6
        plot.show_streamtraces = True

        ribbon = plot.streamtraces.rod_ribbon
        ribbon.width = .008
        ribbon.mesh.show = True
        ribbon.mesh.line_thickness = 0.2
        #Ribbon mesh color inherited from streamtrace color
        plot.streamtraces.color = Color.AquaGreen

        plot.streamtraces.add_rake(
            start_position=(0, 0.22, 0),
            end_position=(0, 0.22, 0.1),
            stream_type=Streamtrace.VolumeRibbon)

        plot.view.width = 0.644
        plot.view.alpha = 66.4
        plot.view.theta = -122.4
        plot.view.psi   = 124.5
        plot.view.position = (5.3, 3.56, -4.3)

        tecplot.export.save_png('streamtrace_ribbon_mesh.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/streamtrace_ribbon_mesh.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/streamtrace_ribbon_mesh.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/streamtrace_ribbon_mesh.png" /></a>
    </figure>

### [StreamtraceRodRibbonShade](#id134){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#streamtracerodribbonshade "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[StreamtraceRodRibbonShade]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[streamtrace]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/streamtrace.html#StreamtraceRodRibbonShade){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonShade "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Color and lighting display for rod/ribbons.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        import os
        import tecplot
        from tecplot.constant import *

        examples_dir = tecplot.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = os.path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'DuctFlow.plt')
        dataset = tecplot.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        frame = tecplot.active_frame()
        frame.plot_type = tecplot.constant.PlotType.Cartesian3D

        plot = frame.plot()
        plot.show_mesh = False
        plot.show_shade = False
        plot.show_edge = True
        plot.fieldmap(0).edge.edge_type = EdgeType.Creases

        plot.vector.u_variable_index = 3
        plot.vector.v_variable_index = 4
        plot.vector.w_variable_index = 5

        plot.show_streamtraces = True
        plot.streamtraces.show_paths = True

        ribbon = plot.streamtraces.rod_ribbon
        ribbon.shade.show = True
        ribbon.shade.color = Color.Blue
        ribbon.shade.use_lighting_effect = True
        ribbon.width = .03


        plot.streamtraces.add_rake(start_position=(1.5, 0, .45),
                                   end_position=(1.5, 1, 0),
                                   stream_type=Streamtrace.VolumeRibbon)

        tecplot.export.save_png('streamtrace_ribbon_shade.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">

    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/streamtrace_ribbon_shade.png" style="width: 300px;"

      [[`show`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonShade.show "tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonShade.show"){.reference .internal}                                                Show shade attributes.
      [[`use_lighting_effect`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonShade.use_lighting_effect "tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonShade.use_lighting_effect"){.reference .internal}   Use lighting effect.

[[StreamtraceRodRibbonShade.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[use_lighting_effect]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceRodRibbonShade.use_lighting_effect "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

    .literal

    .internal} attribute.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    Setting [[`SliceShade.use_lighting_effect`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.SliceShade.use_lighting_effect "tecplot.plot.SliceShade.use_lighting_effect"){.reference

::: {#streamtraceterminationline .section}
### [StreamtraceTerminationLine](#id135){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#streamtraceterminationline "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[StreamtraceTerminationLine]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[streamtrace]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/streamtrace.html#StreamtraceTerminationLine){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Streamtraces termination line attributes.

    A streamtrace termination line is a polyline that terminates any
    streamtraces that cross it. The termination line is useful for
    stopping streamtraces before they spiral or stall.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    Before setting any [[`StreamtraceTerminationLine`{.xref .any .py
    .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine "tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine"){.reference
    .internal} properties, you must [[`add`{.xref .any .py .py-meth
    .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.xref .any .py .py-meth
    .docutils .literal .notranslate}[`a`{.xref .any .py .py-meth
    .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.xref .any .py .py-meth
    .docutils .literal .notranslate}[`termination`{.xref .any .py
    .py-meth .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.xref .any .py
    .py-meth .docutils .literal .notranslate}[`line`{.xref .any .py
    .py-meth .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Streamtraces.set_termination_line "tecplot.plot.Streamtraces.set_termination_line"){.reference
    .internal}.
    :::

    Streamtraces are terminated whenever any of the following occur:

    > <div>
    >
    > - The maximum number of integration steps is reached.
    >
    > - Any point where a streamtrace passes outside the available data.
    >
    > - The streamtrace reaches a point where the velocity magnitude is
    >   zero.
    >
    > </div>

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        import tecplot
        from tecplot.constant import *
        import os

        examples_dir = tecplot.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = os.path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'VortexShedding.plt')
        dataset = tecplot.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        frame = tecplot.active_frame()
        frame.plot_type = tecplot.constant.PlotType.Cartesian2D

        plot = frame.plot()
        plot.vector.u_variable = dataset.variable('U(M/S)')
        plot.vector.v_variable = dataset.variable('V(M/S)')
        plot.show_streamtraces = True
        plot.show_shade = True
        plot.fieldmap(0).shade.color = Color.LightBlue

        streamtraces = plot.streamtraces
        streamtraces.set_termination_line([(0.03, 0.005),
                                           (0.03, -0.005), ])

        term_line = streamtraces.termination_line
        term_line.active = True
        term_line.show = True
        term_line.color = Color.Red
        term_line.line_pattern = LinePattern.Dashed
        term_line.pattern_length = .5
        term_line.line_thickness = .5

        streamtraces.add_rake(start_position=(-0.003, 0.005),
                              end_position=(-0.003, -0.005),
                              stream_type=Streamtrace.TwoDLine,
                              num_seed_points=10)

        plot.axes.y_axis.min = -0.02
        plot.axes.y_axis.max = 0.02
        plot.axes.x_axis.min = -0.01
        plot.axes.x_axis.max = 0.04

        tecplot.export.save_png('streamtrace_term_line.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/streamtrace_term_line.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/streamtrace_term_line.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/streamtrace_term_line.png" /></a>
    </figure>

    **Attributes**

      ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- --------------------------------------------------------------------
      [[`active`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine.active "tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine.active"){.reference .internal}                           Activate/disable the streamtrace termination line.
      [[`color`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine.color "tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine.color"){.reference .internal}                              Color of the termination line.

[[StreamtraceTerminationLine.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[active]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine.active "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Activate/disable the streamtrace termination line.

    Set to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} to activate the termination line and terminate any
    streamtraces that cross it. Set to [[`False`{.xref .any .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#False "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} and redraw the plot with unterminated streamtraces.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    To display the termination line itself, set [[`show`{.xref .any .py
    .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine.show "tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine.show"){.reference
    .internal} to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}.
    :::

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.streamtraces.termination_line.active = True
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[StreamtraceTerminationLine.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[show]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine.show "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Display the termination line.

    Set to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} to display the termination line. Set to [[`False`{.xref
    .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#False "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external} and redraw the plot to display terminated streamlines (if
    [[`active`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine.active "tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine.active"){.reference
    .internal} is set to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}), but not the termination line itself.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    To display terminated streamtraces, [[`active`{.xref .any .py
    .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine.active "tecplot.plot.StreamtraceTerminationLine.active"){.reference
    .internal} must be set to [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}.
    :::

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.streamtraces.termination_line.show = True
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}
:::

### [Font](#id136){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#font "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.text.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[Font]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[parent]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[sv_textshape]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[\'TEXTSHAPE\']{.pre}]{.default_value}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/text/font.html#Font){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.text.Font "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Style of text objects such as titles and labels.

    This class controls the typeface and size of various text objects
    found in plots and axes:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import PlotType, Units, AxisTitleMode

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        infile = path.join(examples_dir, 'OneraM6wing', 'OneraM6_SU2_RANS.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(infile)

        frame = tp.active_frame()
        plot = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian2D)
        plot.activate()

        plot.show_contour = True

        xaxis = plot.axes.x_axis
        xaxis.title.title_mode = AxisTitleMode.UseText
        xaxis.title.text = 'Longitudinal (m)'
        xaxis.min, xaxis.max = 0, 1.2

        yaxis = plot.axes.y_axis
        yaxis.title.title_mode = AxisTitleMode.UseText
        yaxis.title.text = 'Transverse (m)'
        yaxis.min, yaxis.max = 0, 1.3

        for ax in [xaxis, yaxis]:
            ax.title.font.typeface = 'Times'
            ax.title.font.bold = False
            ax.title.font.italic = True
            ax.title.font.size_units = Units.Frame
            ax.title.font.size = 7

        # ensure consistent output between interactive (connected) and batch
        plot.contour(0).levels.reset_to_nice()

        tp.export.save_png('font.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/font.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/font.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/font.png" /></a>
    </figure>

    **Attributes**

      --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- -------------------------------------------------
      [[`bold`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.text.Font.bold "tecplot.text.Font.bold"){.reference .internal}                     Use the bold version of the current typeface.
      [[`italic`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.text.Font.italic "tecplot.text.Font.italic"){.reference .internal}               Use the italic version of the current typeface.
      [[`size`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.text.Font.size "tecplot.text.Font.size"){.reference .internal}                     Height of the font.
      [[`size_units`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.text.Font.size_units "tecplot.text.Font.size_units"){.reference .internal}   Used by the size attribute.
      [[`typeface`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.text.Font.typeface "tecplot.text.Font.typeface"){.reference .internal}         Specific font (or typeface) to use for text.
      --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- -------------------------------------------------

[[TextBox.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[margin]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.text.TextBox.margin "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Margin of the box surrounding the text.

    This property is the margin between the text inside the text box and
    the box as a percentage of frame height.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot = frame.plot()
        >>> plot.legend.box.margin = 0.3
    :::

::: {#labelformat .section}
### [LabelFormat](#id139){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#labelformat "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.text.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[LabelFormat]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[labels]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/text/label_format.html#LabelFormat){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.text.LabelFormat "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Formatting of numbers shown along in axes and in legends.

    This example shows how to format tick label along an axis:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from datetime import datetime
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import PlotType, AxisMode, AxisAlignment, NumberFormat

        tp.new_layout()
        plot = tp.active_frame().plot(tp.constant.PlotType.Sketch)
        plot.activate()

        # setup the plot area margins
        plot.axes.viewport.left = 10
        plot.axes.viewport.right = 90

        # show the x-axis, set the title, and alignment with the viewport
        xaxis = plot.axes.x_axis
        xaxis.show = True
        xaxis.title.text = 'Negative numbers in parentheses'
        xaxis.title.offset = 20
        xaxis.line.alignment = AxisAlignment.WithViewport
        xaxis.line.position = 50

        # set limits, tick placement and tick label properties
        xaxis.ticks.auto_spacing = False
        xaxis.min, xaxis.max = -5.123e-5, 5.234e-5
        xaxis.ticks.spacing = (xaxis.max - xaxis.min) / 6
        xaxis.ticks.spacing_anchor = 0
        xaxis.tick_labels.angle = 45
        xaxis.tick_labels.offset = 3

        # format the tick labels in superscript form. example: 1.234x10^5
        # format negative numbers to use parentheses instead of a negative sign
        xformat = xaxis.tick_labels.format
        xformat.format_type = NumberFormat.SuperScript
        xformat.precision = 3
        xformat.show_negative_sign = False
        xformat.negative_prefix = '('
        xformat.negative_suffix = ')'

        tp.export.save_png('label_format.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">

    src="../_images/label_format.png" style="width: 300px;"

[[LabelFormat.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[add_custom_labels]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[\*]{.pre}]{.o}[[labels]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/text/label_format.html#LabelFormat.add_custom_labels){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.text.LabelFormat.add_custom_labels "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Append a list of custom labels as a new set.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> labels = ['apples', 'bananas', 'carrots']
        >>> axis.tick_labels.format.add_custom_labels(*labels)
        >>> print(axis.tick_labels.format.custom_labels(-1))
        ['apples', 'bananas', 'carrots']
    :::
    ::::

[[LabelFormat.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[datetime_format]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.text.LabelFormat.datetime_format "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   The date/time format to be used.

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import NumberFormat
        >>> axis.tick_labels.format.format_type = NumberFormat.TimeDate
        >>> axis.tick_labels.format.datetime_format = 'mmm d, yyyy'
    :::
    ::::

    The format can be any combination of the following codes. Placing a
    backslash in front of a y, m, d, or s in the Time/Date formula will
    keep it from being processed as part of the formula. All characters
    not part of the Time/Date formula will appear as entered. For
    example, "\\year yyyy" will appear as "year 2008", as the backslash
    keeps the first y from being processed as part of the formula. If
    you use "m" immediately after the "h" or "hh" code or immediately
    before the "ss" code, the minutes instead of the month will be
    displayed.

    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | Years:                                                               |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`yy`{.docutils .literal                 | 00-99                     |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`yyyy`{.docutils .literal               | 1800-9999                 |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | Months:                                                              |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`m`{.docutils .literal                  | 1-12                      |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`mm`{.docutils .literal                 | 01-12                     |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`mmm`{.docutils .literal                | Jan-Dec                   |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`mmmm`{.docutils .literal               | January-December          |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`mmmmm`{.docutils .literal              | first letter of the month |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | Days:                                                                |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`[d]`{.docutils .literal                | elapsed days              |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`d`{.docutils .literal                  | 1-31                      |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`dd`{.docutils .literal                 | 01-31                     |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`ddd`{.docutils .literal                | Sun-Sat                   |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`dddd`{.docutils .literal               | Sunday-Saturday           |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`ddddd`{.docutils .literal              | S,M,T,W,T,F,S             |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | Hours:                                                               |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`[h]`{.docutils .literal                | elapsed hours             |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`h`{.docutils .literal                  | 0-23 or 1-12              |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`hh`{.docutils .literal                 | 00-23 or 1-12             |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`AM/PM`{.docutils .literal              | AM or PM                  |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`A/P`{.docutils .literal                | AM or PM as "A" or "P"    |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | Minutes:                                                             |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`[m]`{.docutils .literal                | elapsed minutes           |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`m`{.docutils .literal                  | 0-59                      |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`mm`{.docutils .literal                 | 00-59                     |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | Seconds:                                 |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`s`{.docutils .literal                  | 0-59                      |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`ss`{.docutils .literal                 | 00-59                     |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`.0`{.docutils .literal                 | Tenths                    |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`.00`{.docutils .literal                | Hundredths                |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+
    | [`.000`{.docutils .literal               | Thousandths               |
    | .notranslate}]{.pre}                     |                           |
    +------------------------------------------+---------------------------+

    To display the time and date on your plot as a "Sat-Jan-05-2008",
    enter the following code:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        "ddd-mmm-dd-yyyy"
    :::
    ::::

    To display the time and date on your plot as a "1-3-08", enter the
    following code:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        "m-d-yy"
    :::
    ::::

    To display the time and date on your plot as a "9:30:05 AM", enter
    the following code:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        "h:mm:ss AM"
    :::
    ::::

    To display an elapsed time, such as "3:10:15", enter the following
    code:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        "[d]:hh:mm"
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`str`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#str "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

### [FieldPlotDataLabels](#id140){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#fieldplotdatalabels "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[FieldPlotDataLabels]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/labels.html#FieldPlotDataLabels){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.FieldPlotDataLabels "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Node and cell labels for field plots.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import LabelType, NumberFormat, PlotType

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        infile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'RainierElevation.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(infile)

        frame = tp.active_frame()
        plot = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian2D)
        plot.activate()
        plot.show_contour = True
        plot.contour(0).legend.show = False

        plot.axes.x_axis.min = -8500
        plot.axes.x_axis.max = 8200
        plot.axes.y_axis.min = -400
        plot.axes.y_axis.max = -150

        plot.data_labels.show_node_labels = True
        plot.data_labels.node_label_type = LabelType.VarValue
        plot.data_labels.node_variable = dataset.variable('E')
        plot.data_labels.index_step = 4
        plot.data_labels.label_format.format_type = NumberFormat.Integer
        plot.data_labels.show_box = False

        tp.export.save_png('field_plot_data_labels.png')
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/field_plot_data_labels.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/field_plot_data_labels.png" style="width: 300px;"

    </figure>

[[FieldPlotDataLabels.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[color_by_map]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.FieldPlotDataLabels.color_by_map "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Inherit [[`Color`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Color "tecplot.constant.Color"){.reference
    .internal} from the symbol or scatter mapping style.

    Example usage for linemaps:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import Color
        >>> plot.linemap(0).symbols.color = Color.Blue
        >>> plot.data_labels.show_node_labels = True
        >>> plot.data_labels.color_by_map = True
    :::
    ::::

    Example usage for fieldmaps:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import Color
        >>> plot.fieldmap(0).scatter.color = Color.Yellow
        >>> plot.data_labels.show_node_labels = True
        >>> plot.data_labels.color_by_map = True
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference

[[FieldPlotDataLabels.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[index_step]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.FieldPlotDataLabels.index_step "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Step interval between labels.

    A value of 1 displays labels on all nodes or cells. Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.data_labels.show_node_labels = True
        >>> plot.data_labels.index_step = 10
    :::

### [LinePlotDataLabels](#id141){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#lineplotdatalabels "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[LinePlotDataLabels]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/labels.html#LinePlotDataLabels){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.LinePlotDataLabels "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Node labels for line plots.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import PlotType

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        infile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'SunSpots.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(infile)

        frame = tp.active_frame()
        plot = frame.plot(PlotType.XYLine)
        plot.activate()
        plot.data_labels.show_node_labels = True
        plot.data_labels.index_step = 3

        tp.export.save_png('line_plot_data_labels.png')
    :::
    ::::

[[LinePlotDataLabels.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[color_by_map]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.LinePlotDataLabels.color_by_map "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Inherit [[`Color`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Color "tecplot.constant.Color"){.reference
    .internal} from the symbol or scatter mapping style.

    Example usage for linemaps:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import Color
        >>> plot.linemap(0).symbols.color = Color.Blue
        >>> plot.data_labels.show_node_labels = True
        >>> plot.data_labels.color_by_map = True
    :::
    ::::

    Example usage for fieldmaps:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import Color
        >>> plot.fieldmap(0).scatter.color = Color.Yellow
        >>> plot.data_labels.show_node_labels = True
        >>> plot.data_labels.color_by_map = True
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

- [FieldPlotHOESettings](#fieldplothoesettings){#id142 .reference
  .internal}

::: {#fieldplothoesettings .section}
### [FieldPlotHOESettings](#id142){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#fieldplothoesettings "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[FieldPlotHOESettings]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[frame]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/hoe_settings.html#FieldPlotHOESettings){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.FieldPlotHOESettings "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   **Attributes**

      ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- ------------------------------------------------------------
      [[`minmax_scaling_factor`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.FieldPlotHOESettings.minmax_scaling_factor "tecplot.plot.FieldPlotHOESettings.minmax_scaling_factor"){.reference .internal}      Widens or narrows the buffer around sub-element filtering.
      [[`num_subdivision_levels`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.FieldPlotHOESettings.num_subdivision_levels "tecplot.plot.FieldPlotHOESettings.num_subdivision_levels"){.reference .internal}   Refine element decomposition.
      ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- ------------------------------------------------------------

<!-- -->

[[FieldPlotHOESettings.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[minmax_scaling_factor]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.FieldPlotHOESettings.minmax_scaling_factor "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Widens or narrows the buffer around sub-element filtering.

    The default value is 1.0. When filtering sub-elements, the extrema
    (min/max) of an iso-surface or probe variable for the nodes of an
    sub-element is multiplied by the factor and added as a buffer around
    the variable min/max range. Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.hoe_settings.minmax_scaling_factor = 4.0
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[FieldPlotHOESettings.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[num_subdivision_levels]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.FieldPlotHOESettings.num_subdivision_levels "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Refine element decomposition.

    With a default setting of 1, the elements are subdivided using only
    the natural nodes. A value of 2 subdivides elements one level below
    the natural nodes. Greater than 2 further refines the element
    decomposition and is more accurate, using the basis functions to
    compute the manufactured sub-element node values. Each level of
    subdivisions increases accuracy and time required to render. As a
    special case, a value of zero is permitted, instructing Tecplot to
    ignore the high order natural nodes using only the corner nodes for
    rendering. Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.hoe_settings.num_subdivision_levels = 3
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`int`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}
:::
::::

### [Cartesian2DFieldView](#id147){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#cartesian2dfieldview "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[Cartesian2DFieldView]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[\*]{.pre}]{.o}[[svargs]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/view.html#Cartesian2DFieldView){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Adjust the way cartesian 2D data is displayed.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import PlotType

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        infile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'HeatExchanger.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(infile)

        frame = tp.active_frame()
        plot = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian2D)
        plot.activate()
        plot.show_contour = True
        plot.contour(0).variable = dataset.variable('P(N)')
        plot.contour(0).colormap_name = 'Sequential - Yellow/Green/Blue'

        plot.view.fit_to_nice()

        # ensure consistent output between interactive (connected) and batch
        plot.contour(0).levels.reset_to_nice()

        tp.export.save_png('view_2D.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/view_2D.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/view_2D.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/view_2D.png" /></a>
    </figure>

    **Attributes**

      -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- -------------------------------------------

    **Methods**

      [[`fit`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.fit "tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.fit"){.reference .internal}(\[consider_blanking\])                           Fit the data being plotted within the axis grid area.
      [[`fit_data`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.fit_data "tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.fit_data"){.reference .internal}(\[consider_blanking\])            Fit data zones or line mappings within the grid area.
      [[`fit_to_nice`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.fit_to_nice "tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.fit_to_nice"){.reference .internal}(\[consider_blanking\])   Set axis range to begin/end on major axis increments.
      [[`translate`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.translate "tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.translate"){.reference .internal}(\[x, y\])                      Shift the data being plotted in the X and/or Y direction.
      [[`zoom`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.zoom "tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.zoom"){.reference .internal}(xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)                       Zoom the view to a rectangular region of the plot.

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian2DFieldView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[adjust_to_nice]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.adjust_to_nice "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Shifts axes to make axis-line values "nice"

    Modifies the axis range to fit the minimum and maximum of the
    variable assigned to that axis, then snaps the major tick marks to
    the ends of the axis. If axis dependency is not independent, this
    may affect the range on another axis.

    In other words, given an existing range of values RMin, RMax and an
    initial delta, D (such as axis ranges with grid spacing or contour
    levels), determine a new delta (ND) that:

    - Is 1,2, or 5 times 10 to some power that is the "best" alternative
      to D.

    - Produces new range min and max values that are some multiple of ND
      that are nearest the original RMin and RMax

    Axes are shifted without changing the extents of the window.

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian2DFieldView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[center]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[consider_blanking]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[True]{.pre}]{.default_value}*[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.center "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

        .external})

[[Cartesian2DFieldView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[magnification]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.magnification "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Magnification for the data being plotted.

    The [`magnification`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} value
    is a decimal percent and must be greater than 0. A
    [`magnification`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} size of 1.0
    (100%) will size the plot so that it can fit within the grid area.
    The following example will scale the view to ten percent of the size
    at which the data would fit the full frame:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.magnification = 0.10
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian2DFieldView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[translate]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[x]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[0.0]{.pre}]{.default_value}*, *[[y]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[0.0]{.pre}]{.default_value}*[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.translate "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

    :::

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   - **x** ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}, optional) -- Amount to shift in the X direction as
          a percentage of the frame width. Positive values shift right,
          negative values shift left. (default: 0.0)

        - **y** ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}, optional) -- Amount to shift in the Y direction as
          a percentage of the frame height. Positive values shift up,
          negative values shift down. (default: 0.0)

    Translate the view 10 percent of the frame width to the right:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.translate(x=10)
    :::
    ::::

    Translate the view 5 percent of the frame width to the right, 20 of
    the frame height down:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.translate(x=5, y=-20)
    :::
    ::::

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian2DFieldView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[zoom]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[xmin]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[ymin]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[xmax]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[ymax]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian2DFieldView.zoom "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Zoom the view to a rectangular region of the plot.

    Change the view by "zooming" into the data. Ranges on the axes are
    adjusted to view the region defined by the rectangle with corners at
    ([`xmin`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}, [`ymin`{.docutils
    .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}) and ([`xmax`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}, [`ymax`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}).

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    All position values are defined in units of the X- and Y- axis (that
    is, grid coordinates).
    :::

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   - **xmin** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) X min corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

        - **ymin** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) Y min corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

        - **xmax** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) X max corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

        - **ymax** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) Y max corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

    Zoom so the rectangular region with corners at [`(1,`{.docutils
    .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`0)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} and
    [`(7,`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`9)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} is in
    view:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.zoom(1, 7, 0, 9)
    :::
    ::::
:::

::: {#cartesian3dview .section}
### [Cartesian3DView](#id148){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#cartesian3dview "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[Cartesian3DView]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/view.html#Cartesian3DView){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Adjust the way cartesian 3D data is displayed.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        import tecplot
        import os
        from tecplot.constant import *
        examples_dir = tecplot.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        infile = os.path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'F18.plt')
        ds = tecplot.data.load_tecplot(infile)
        plot = tecplot.active_frame().plot(PlotType.Cartesian3D)
        plot.activate()
        plot.view.width = 17.5
        plot.view.alpha = 0
        plot.view.theta = 125
        plot.view.psi   = 65
        plot.view.position = (-100, 80, 65)

        tecplot.export.save_jpeg('view_3D.jpeg', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/view_3D.jpeg"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/view_3D.jpeg" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/view_3D.jpeg" /></a>
    </figure>

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[alpha]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.alpha "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Eye origin view Alpha angle in degrees.

    The Alpha angle is the twist about the eye origin ray:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.view.alpha = 95.0
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[center]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[consider_blanking]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[True]{.pre}]{.default_value}*[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.center "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Center the data within the axis grid area.

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   **consider_blanking** ([[`Boolean`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}, optional) -- If [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils
        .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external} and blanking is enabled, the resulting view excludes
        blanked cells at the edges of the plot. If 'False\`, then the
        resulting view will ignore blanked cells at the edges of the
        plot. (default: [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external})

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[distance]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.distance "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Get or set the view distance.

    The view distance is the distance from the viewer to the plane that
    is parallel to the screen and passes through the 3-D rotation
    origin.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    Changing this value will also change the viewer [[`position`{.xref
    .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.position "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.position"){.reference
    .internal}.
    :::

    ::: {.admonition .seealso}
    See also

    [[`position`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.position "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.position"){.reference
    .internal}
    :::

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.view.distance
        13.5
        >>> plot.view.distance = 10.0
        >>> plot.view.distance
        10.0
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[field_of_view]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.field_of_view "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Amount of the plot which is displayed.

    Get or set the amount of the plot (in terms of spherical arc) in
    front of the viewer that may be seen.

    ::: {.admonition .warning}
    Warning

    [`field_of_view`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} cannot be
    set if [[`projection`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.projection "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.projection"){.reference
    .internal} is [[`Projection.Orthographic`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Projection.Orthographic "tecplot.constant.Projection.Orthographic"){.reference
    .internal}.
    :::

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import Projection
        >>> plot.view.projection = Projection.Perspective
        >>> plot.view.field_of_view = 9.6
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[fit]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[consider_blanking]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[True]{.pre}]{.default_value}*[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.fit "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    This also takes into consideration text and geometries that are
    plotted using the grid coordinate system.
    :::

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   **consider_blanking** ([[`Boolean`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}, optional) -- If [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils
        .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external} and blanking is enabled, the resulting view excludes
        blanked cells at the edges of the plot. If 'False\`, then the
        resulting view will ignore blanked cells at the edges of the
        plot. (default: [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external})

:   Fit data zones or line mappings within the grid area.

        .literal

        .external} and blanking is enabled, the resulting view excludes
        blanked cells at the edges of the plot. If 'False\`, then the

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[fit_surfaces]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[consider_blanking]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[True]{.pre}]{.default_value}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/view.html#Cartesian3DView.fit_surfaces){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.fit_surfaces "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   **consider_blanking** ([[`bool`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#bool "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}, optional) -- If [[`True`{.xref .any .docutils
        .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/constants.html#True "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external} and blanking is enabled, the resulting view excludes
        blanked cells at the edges of the plot. If 'False\`, then the
        resulting view will ignore blanked cells at the edges of the

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[magnification]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.magnification "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Magnification for the data being plotted.

    The [`magnification`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} value
    is a decimal percent and must be greater than 0. A
    [`magnification`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} size of 1.0
    (100%) will size the plot so that it can fit within the grid area.
    The following example will scale the view to ten percent of the size
    at which the data would fit the full frame:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.magnification = 0.10
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[position]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.position "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   3D viewer position.

    The viewer position is the viewer's relation to the image:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.view.position
        (1.25, 3.2, 0.74)
        >>> plot.view.position.x
        1.25
        >>> plot.view.position = (2.5, 0.0, 1.0)
        >>> plot.view.position.y
        0.0
        >>> plot.view.position.z
        1.0
    :::
    ::::

    ::: {.admonition .seealso}
    See also

    [[`distance`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.distance "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.distance"){.reference
    .internal}
    :::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[projection]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.projection "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Projection type ([[`Perspective`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Projection.Perspective "tecplot.constant.Projection.Perspective"){.reference
    .internal} or [[`Orthographic`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Projection.Orthographic "tecplot.constant.Projection.Orthographic"){.reference
    .internal}).

    When set to [[`Perspective`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Projection.Perspective "tecplot.constant.Projection.Perspective"){.reference
    .internal}, Tecplot 360 draws the plot in perspective. When set to
    [[`Orthographic`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Projection.Orthographic "tecplot.constant.Projection.Orthographic"){.reference
    .internal}, the plot is drawn with orthographic projection where the
    shape of the object does not change with distance.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    Requires Tecplot version 2017.2 or later.
    :::

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> from tecplot.constant import Projection
        >>> plot.view.projection = Projection.Orthographic
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`Projection`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Projection "tecplot.constant.Projection"){.reference
        .internal}

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[psi]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.psi "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Eye origin view Psi angle in degrees.

    The Psi angle is the tilt of the eye origin ray away from the
    Z-axis:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.view.psi = 90.0
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[rotate_axes]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[angle]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[normal]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[(0,]{.pre} [0,]{.pre} [1)]{.pre}]{.default_value}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/view.html#Cartesian3DView.rotate_axes){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.rotate_axes "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Adjust the view so the axes are rotated about some normal vector.

    This effectively rotates the axes around the axes origin about some
    **normal** using the right-hand rule by the specified **angle** in
    degrees. The rotation is performed about the position:
    [[`Cartesian3DView.rotation_origin`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.rotation_origin "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.rotation_origin"){.reference
    .internal}.

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   - **angle** ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) -- The angle in degrees to rotate.

        - **normal** ([[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}, optional) -- The direction vector [\\((x, y,
          z)\\)]{.math .notranslate .nohighlight} around which the
          rotation will be done. (default: [`(0,`{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}[`0,`{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}[`1)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre})

    Example of rotating 30 degrees about the [\\(x\\)]{.math
    .notranslate .nohighlight}-axis and around the data's origin:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.view.rotation_origin = (0, 0, 0)
        >>> plot.view.rotate_axes(30, (1, 0, 0))
    :::
    ::::

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[rotate_to_angles]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[psi]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[theta]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[alpha]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/view.html#Cartesian3DView.rotate_to_angles){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.rotate_to_angles "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Rotate the plot to specific angles.

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   - **psi** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}): Tilt, in degrees, of the eye origin ray away from
          the Z-axis.

        - **theta** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}): Rotation, in degrees, of the eye origin ray about
          the Z-axis.

        - **alpha** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}): Twist, in degrees, about the eye origin ray.

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[rotate_viewer]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[angle]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[normal]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[(0,]{.pre} [0,]{.pre} [1)]{.pre}]{.default_value}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/view.html#Cartesian3DView.rotate_viewer){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.rotate_viewer "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Rotate the camera or viewer about some normal vector.

    This rotates the viewer about some **normal** using the right-hand
    rule by the specified **angle** in degrees. The rotation is
    performed about the position:
    [[`Cartesian3DView.rotation_origin`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.rotation_origin "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.rotation_origin"){.reference
    .internal}.

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   - **angle** ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) -- The angle in degrees to rotate.

        - **normal** ([[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}, optional) -- The direction vector [\\((x, y,
          z)\\)]{.math .notranslate .nohighlight} around which the
          rotation will be done. (default: [`(0,`{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}[`0,`{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
          .notranslate}[`1)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre})

    Example of rotating 2 degrees about the [\\(x\\)]{.math .notranslate
    .nohighlight}-axis and around the data's origin:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.view.rotation_origin = (0, 0, 0)
        >>> plot.view.rotate_viewer(2, (1, 0, 0))
    :::
    ::::

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[rotation_origin]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.rotation_origin "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[theta]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.theta "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Eye origin view Theta angle in degrees.

    The Theta angle is the rotation of the eye origin ray about the
    Z-axis:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.view.theta = 24.3
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
