[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[translate]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[x]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[0.0]{.pre}]{.default_value}*, *[[y]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[0.0]{.pre}]{.default_value}*[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.translate "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Shift the data being plotted in the X and/or Y direction.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    The amount translated is in frame units.
    :::

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   - **x** ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}, optional) -- Amount to shift in the X direction as
          a percentage of the frame width. Positive values shift right,
          negative values shift left. (default: 0.0)

        - **y** ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}, optional) -- Amount to shift in the Y direction as
          a percentage of the frame height. Positive values shift up,
          negative values shift down. (default: 0.0)

    Translate the view 10 percent of the frame width to the right:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.translate(x=10)
    :::
    ::::

    Translate the view 5 percent of the frame width to the right, 20 of
    the frame height down:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.translate(x=5, y=-20)
    :::
    ::::

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[width]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.width "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   3D view width.

    The 3D view width is the amount of the plot (in X-axis units) in
    front of the viewer that may be seen.

    ::: {.admonition .warning}
    Warning

    [[`width`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.width "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.width"){.reference
    .internal} cannot be set if [[`projection`{.xref .any .py .py-attr
    .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.projection "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.projection"){.reference
    .internal} is [[`Perspective`{.xref .any .py .py-attr .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.constants.html#tecplot.constant.Projection.Perspective "tecplot.constant.Projection.Perspective"){.reference
    .internal}.
    :::

    Example usage:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.view.width = 1.5
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[Cartesian3DView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[zoom]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[xmin]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[ymin]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[xmax]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[ymax]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DView.zoom "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Zoom the view to a rectangular region of the plot.

    Change the view by "zooming" into the data. Ranges on the axes are
    adjusted to view the region defined by the rectangle with corners at
    ([`xmin`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}, [`ymin`{.docutils
    .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}) and ([`xmax`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}, [`ymax`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}).

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    All position values are defined in units of the X- and Y- axis (that
    is, grid coordinates).
    :::

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   - **xmin** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) X min corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

        - **ymin** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) Y min corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

        - **xmax** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) X max corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

        - **ymax** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) Y max corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

    Zoom so the rectangular region with corners at [`(1,`{.docutils
    .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`0)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} and
    [`(7,`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`9)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} is in
    view:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.zoom(1, 7, 0, 9)
    :::
    ::::
:::

::: {#xylineview .section}
### [XYLineView](#id149){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#xylineview "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[XYLineView]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/view.html#XYLineView){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Adjust the way XY Line data is displayed.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        import os
        import tecplot
        from tecplot.constant import *

        examples_dir = tecplot.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        datafile = os.path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'Rainfall.dat')
        dataset = tecplot.data.load_tecplot(datafile)

        frame = tecplot.active_frame()
        plot = frame.plot()
        frame.plot_type = tecplot.constant.PlotType.XYLine

        for i in range(3):
            plot.linemap(i).show = True
            plot.linemap(i).line.line_thickness = .4

        y_axis = plot.axes.y_axis(0)
        y_axis.title.title_mode = AxisTitleMode.UseText
        y_axis.title.text = 'Rainfall (in)'
        plot.view.fit_to_nice()

        tecplot.export.save_png('view_line.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/view_line.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/view_line.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/view_line.png" /></a>
    </figure>

    **Attributes**

      [[`magnification`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.magnification "tecplot.plot.XYLineView.magnification"){.reference .internal}   Magnification for the data being plotted.
      ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ -------------------------------------------

    **Methods**

      ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- -----------------------------------------------------------
      [[`adjust_to_nice`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.adjust_to_nice "tecplot.plot.XYLineView.adjust_to_nice"){.reference .internal}()   Shifts axes to make axis-line values \"nice\"
      [[`center`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.center "tecplot.plot.XYLineView.center"){.reference .internal}()                           Center the data within the axis grid area.
      [[`fit`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.fit "tecplot.plot.XYLineView.fit"){.reference .internal}()                                    Fit the data being plotted within the axis grid area.
      [[`fit_data`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.fit_data "tecplot.plot.XYLineView.fit_data"){.reference .internal}()                     Fit data zones or line mappings within the grid area.
      [[`fit_to_nice`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.fit_to_nice "tecplot.plot.XYLineView.fit_to_nice"){.reference .internal}()            Set axis range to begin/end on major axis increments.
      [[`translate`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.translate "tecplot.plot.XYLineView.translate"){.reference .internal}(\[x, y\])          Shift the data being plotted in the X and/or Y direction.
      [[`zoom`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.zoom "tecplot.plot.XYLineView.zoom"){.reference .internal}(xmin, ymin, xmax, ymax)           Zoom the view to a rectangular region of the plot.
      ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- -----------------------------------------------------------

<!-- -->

[[XYLineView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[adjust_to_nice]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.adjust_to_nice "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Shifts axes to make axis-line values "nice"

    Modifies the axis range to fit the minimum and maximum of the
    variable assigned to that axis, then snaps the major tick marks to
    the ends of the axis. If axis dependency is not independent, this
    may affect the range on another axis.

    In other words, given an existing range of values RMin, RMax and an
    initial delta, D (such as axis ranges with grid spacing or contour
    levels), determine a new delta (ND) that:

    - Is 1,2, or 5 times 10 to some power that is the "best" alternative
      to D.

    - Produces new range min and max values that are some multiple of ND
      that are nearest the original RMin and RMax

    Axes are shifted without changing the extents of the window.

<!-- -->

[[XYLineView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[center]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.center "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

        .internal} -- View could not be centered.

[[XYLineView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[extents]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.extents "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Viewport extents in data units.

    Extents are represented by the [[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}: [`(x1,`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`y1,`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`x2,`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`y2)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} and
    setting this effectively zooms the view of the data within the
    viewport. The values [`(x1,`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`y1)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} and
    [`(x2,`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils
    .literal .notranslate}[`y2)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}
    are the lower-left and upper-right edges respectively and are in
    data units. The following example will set the bottom and left edges
    of the viewport to the value of [`-3`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre} and the top and right edges to the value of 5:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.view.extents = -3, -3, 5, 5
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[XYLineView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[fit_to_nice]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.fit_to_nice "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

    plot with integer values for axis labels.

[[XYLineView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[magnification]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.magnification "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Magnification for the data being plotted.

    The [`magnification`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} value
    is a decimal percent and must be greater than 0. A
    [`magnification`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} size of 1.0
    (100%) will size the plot so that it can fit within the grid area.
    The following example will scale the view to ten percent of the size
    at which the data would fit the full frame:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.magnification = 0.10
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[XYLineView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[translate]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[x]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[0.0]{.pre}]{.default_value}*, *[[y]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[0.0]{.pre}]{.default_value}*[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.translate "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Shift the data being plotted in the X and/or Y direction.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    The amount translated is in frame units.
    :::

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   - **x** ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}, optional) -- Amount to shift in the X direction as
          a percentage of the frame width. Positive values shift right,
          negative values shift left. (default: 0.0)

        - **y** ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}, optional) -- Amount to shift in the Y direction as
          a percentage of the frame height. Positive values shift up,
          negative values shift down. (default: 0.0)

    Translate the view 10 percent of the frame width to the right:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.translate(x=10)
    :::
    ::::

    Translate the view 5 percent of the frame width to the right, 20 of
    the frame height down:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.translate(x=5, y=-20)
    :::
    ::::

<!-- -->

[[XYLineView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[zoom]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[xmin]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[ymin]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[xmax]{.pre}]{.n}*, *[[ymax]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.XYLineView.zoom "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Zoom the view to a rectangular region of the plot.

    Change the view by "zooming" into the data. Ranges on the axes are
    adjusted to view the region defined by the rectangle with corners at
    ([`xmin`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}, [`ymin`{.docutils
    .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}) and ([`xmax`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}, [`ymax`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}).

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    All position values are defined in units of the X- and Y- axis (that
    is, grid coordinates).
    :::

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   - **xmin** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) X min corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

        - **ymin** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) Y min corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

        - **xmax** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) X max corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

        - **ymax** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) Y max corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

    Zoom so the rectangular region with corners at [`(1,`{.docutils
    .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`0)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} and
    [`(7,`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`9)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} is in
    view:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.zoom(1, 7, 0, 9)
    :::
    ::::
:::

::: {#polarview .section}
### [PolarView](#id150){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#polarview "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[PolarView]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/view.html#PolarView){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.PolarView "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Adjust the way polar data is displayed.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        import numpy as np
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import PlotType, ThetaMode

        frame = tp.active_frame()

        npoints = 300
        r = np.linspace(0, 2000, npoints)
        theta = np.linspace(0, 10, npoints)

        dataset = frame.create_dataset('Data', ['R', 'Theta'])
        zone = dataset.add_ordered_zone('Zone', (300,))
        zone.values('R')[:] = r
        zone.values('Theta')[:] = theta

        plot = frame.plot(PlotType.PolarLine)
        plot.activate()

        plot.axes.r_axis.max = np.max(r)
        plot.axes.theta_axis.mode = ThetaMode.Radians

        plot.delete_linemaps()
        lmap = plot.add_linemap('Linemap', zone, dataset.variable('R'),
                                dataset.variable('Theta'))
        lmap.line.line_thickness = 0.8

        plot.view.fit()

        tp.export.save_png('view_polar.png', 600, supersample=3)
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/view_polar.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/view_polar.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/view_polar.png" /></a>
    </figure>

      [[`magnification`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.PolarView.magnification "tecplot.plot.PolarView.magnification"){.reference .internal}   Magnification for the data being plotted.
      ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- -------------------------------------------

      [[`fit_data`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.PolarView.fit_data "tecplot.plot.PolarView.fit_data"){.reference .internal}()              Fit data zones or line mappings within the grid area.

[[PolarView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[center]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.PolarView.center "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

        .internal} -- View could not be centered.

[[PolarView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[extents]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.PolarView.extents "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Viewport extents in radial data units.

    Extents are represented by the [[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils
    .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
    .external}: [`(x1,`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`y1,`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`x2,`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`y2)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} and
    setting this effectively zooms the view of the data within the
    viewport. The values [`(x1,`{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`y1)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} and
    [`(x2,`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils
    .literal .notranslate}[`y2)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}
    are the lower-left and upper-right edges respectively and are in
    radial (data) units. The following example will set the bottom and
    left edges of the viewport to the radial value of [`-3`{.docutils
    .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} and the top and right edges to the
    radial value of 5:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.view.extents = -3, -3, 5, 5
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`tuple`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#tuple "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[PolarView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[fit]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.PolarView.fit "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Fit the data being plotted within the axis grid area.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    This also takes into consideration text and geometries that are
    plotted using the grid coordinate system.
    :::

<!-- -->

[[PolarView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[fit_data]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.PolarView.fit_data "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Fit data zones or line mappings within the grid area.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    This does not take into consideration text and geometries that are
    plotted using the grid coordinate system.
    :::

<!-- -->

[[PolarView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[magnification]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.PolarView.magnification "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Magnification for the data being plotted.

    The [`magnification`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} value
    is a decimal percent and must be greater than 0. A
    [`magnification`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} size of 1.0
    (100%) will size the plot so that it can fit within the grid area.
    The following example will scale the view to ten percent of the size
    at which the data would fit the full frame:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.magnification = 0.10
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}

<!-- -->

[[PolarView.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[translate]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[x]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[0.0]{.pre}]{.default_value}*, *[[y]{.pre}]{.n}[[=]{.pre}]{.o}[[0.0]{.pre}]{.default_value}*[)]{.sig-paren}[¶](#tecplot.plot.PolarView.translate "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Shift the data being plotted in the X and/or Y direction.

    ::: {.admonition .note}
    Note

    The amount translated is in frame units.
    :::

    Parameters[:]{.colon}

    :   - **x** ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}, optional) -- Amount to shift in the X direction as
          a percentage of the frame width. Positive values shift right,
          negative values shift left. (default: 0.0)

        - **y** ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}, optional) -- Amount to shift in the Y direction as
          a percentage of the frame height. Positive values shift up,
          negative values shift down. (default: 0.0)

    Translate the view 10 percent of the frame width to the right:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.translate(x=10)
    :::
    ::::

    Translate the view 5 percent of the frame width to the right, 20 of
    the frame height down:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> view.translate(x=5, y=-20)
    :::
    ::::

<!-- -->

          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) X min corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

        - **ymin** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) Y min corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

        - **xmax** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) X max corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

        - **ymax** -- ([[`float`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
          .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#float "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
          .external}) Y max corner of the rectangle to be viewed.

    Zoom so the rectangular region with corners at [`(1,`{.docutils
    .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`0)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} and
    [`(7,`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}` `{.docutils .literal
    .notranslate}[`9)`{.docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre} is in
    view:

    ::::

::: {#lightsource .section}
### [LightSource](#id151){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#lightsource "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[LightSource]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[plot]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/effects.html#LightSource){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.LightSource "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Three-dimensional light source style control.

    The light source is a point of light infinitely far from the drawing
    area.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        from os import path
        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import PlotType, Color

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        infile = path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'F18.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(infile)

        frame = tp.active_frame()
        plot = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian3D)
        plot.activate()

        plot.light_source.direction = (0., -0.7, 0.9)
        plot.light_source.intensity = 70
        plot.light_source.specular_intensity = 80
        plot.light_source.specular_shininess = 50

        tp.export.save_png('light_source.png')
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/light_source.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/light_source.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/light_source.png" /></a>
    </figure>

      [[`force_paneled_for_cell_flood`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.LightSource.force_paneled_for_cell_flood "tecplot.plot.LightSource.force_paneled_for_cell_flood"){.reference .internal}            Force paneled effects for shaded cell flooding.
      [[`intensity`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.LightSource.intensity "tecplot.plot.LightSource.intensity"){.reference .internal}                                                                     Percentage intensity of the light source.

[[LightSource.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[direction]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.LightSource.direction "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   [\\((x, y, z)\\)]{.math .notranslate .nohighlight} direction of the
    light rays.

    The direction is in the view coordinate system where [\\(z\\)]{.math
    .notranslate .nohighlight} goes into the page and the origin of
    [\\((x, y)\\)]{.math .notranslate .nohighlight} is in the lower left
    corner. The default is [\\((-0.2, -0.2, 0.959)\\)]{.math
    .notranslate .nohighlight}:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> plot.light_source.direction = (0, -0.7, 0.9)
    :::
    ::::

### [Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames](#id154){.toc-backref role="doc-backlink"}[¶](#cartesian3dplotlinkingbetweenframes "Link to this heading"){.headerlink}

*[class]{.pre}[ ]{.w}*[[tecplot.plot.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[(]{.sig-paren}*[[frame]{.pre}]{.n}*[)]{.sig-paren}[[[\[source\]]{.pre}]{.viewcode-link}](../_modules/tecplot/plot/linking.html#Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames){.reference .internal}[¶](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   [[`Cartesian3DFieldPlot`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.plot.html#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DFieldPlot "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DFieldPlot"){.reference
    .internal} [[`Frame`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.layout.html#tecplot.layout.Frame "tecplot.layout.Frame"){.reference
    .internal} style linking control.

    The following example shows how to set up a series of transparent
    overlay frames where each overlay shows one component of the [\\((U,
    V, W)\\)]{.math .notranslate .nohighlight} vector from the
    [[`Dataset`{.xref .any .py .py-class .docutils .literal
    .notranslate}]{.pre}](tecplot.data.html#tecplot.data.Dataset "tecplot.data.Dataset"){.reference
    .internal}. All four frames are linked to each other (group 1) so
    they have the same size, position and view.

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        import os

        import tecplot as tp
        from tecplot.constant import *

        examples_dir = tp.session.tecplot_examples_directory()
        infile = os.path.join(examples_dir, 'SimpleData', 'DuctFlow.plt')
        dataset = tp.data.load_tecplot(infile)

        # Create a "blank" (zeroed-out) variable to use when plotting
        # only one component of the (U, V, W) vectors
        tp.data.operate.execute_equation(r'{blank} = 0')

        # Setup the background frame and plot style
        frame = tp.active_frame()
        frame.background_color = Color.Black

        plot = frame.plot(PlotType.Cartesian3D)
        plot.activate()

        contour = plot.contour(0)
        contour.variable = dataset.variable('P(N/m2)')
        contour.legend.show = False

        plot.use_translucency = True
        plot.show_contour = True
        plot.show_edge = True
        plot.axes.orientation_axis.color = Color.White
        plot.view.width = 2.43

        fmap = plot.fieldmap(0)
        fmap.edge.edge_type = EdgeType.Creases
        fmap.edge.color = Color.White
        fmap.surfaces.surfaces_to_plot = SurfacesToPlot.BoundaryFaces

        frame_linking = plot.linking_between_frames
        frame_linking.group = 1
        frame_linking.link_view = True
        frame_linking.link_frame_size_and_position = True

        def add_transparent_overlay(frame):
            '''Creates a transparent frame overlay with "blank" vector variables.'''
            overlay_frame = frame.page.add_frame()
            overlay_frame.transparent = True

            plot = overlay_frame.plot(frame.plot_type)
            plot.activate()
            plot.show_shade = False
            plot.axes.orientation_axis.show = False

            blank_var = overlay_frame.dataset.variable('blank')
            plot.vector.u_variable = blank_var
            plot.vector.v_variable = blank_var
            plot.vector.w_variable = blank_var
            plot.show_vector = True

            fmap = plot.fieldmap(0)
            fmap.vector.line_thickness = 0.35
            fmap.points.step = 80
            fmap.points.points_to_plot = PointsToPlot.AllCellCenters

            frame_linking = plot.linking_between_frames
            frame_linking.group = 1
            frame_linking.link_view = True
            frame_linking.link_frame_size_and_position = True

            return plot

        # Create three overlays - one for each vector component we want to show
        u_plot = add_transparent_overlay(frame)
        u_plot.vector.u_variable = dataset.variable('U(M/S)')
        u_plot.fieldmap(0).vector.color = Color.Red

        v_plot = add_transparent_overlay(frame)
        v_plot.vector.v_variable = dataset.variable('V(M/S)')
        v_plot.fieldmap(0).vector.color = Color.Green

        w_plot = add_transparent_overlay(frame)
        w_plot.vector.w_variable = dataset.variable('W(M/S)')
        w_plot.fieldmap(0).vector.color = Color.Blue

        # Now that all plots have been linked,
        # movement in one will affect all three plots.
        u_plot.view.translate(x=5)

        tp.export.save_png('plot3d_linking_between_frames.png')
    :::
    ::::

    <figure class="align-default" style="width: 300px">
    <a href="../_images/plot3d_linking_between_frames.png"
    class="reference internal image-reference"><img
    src="../_images/plot3d_linking_between_frames.png" style="width: 300px;"
    alt="../_images/plot3d_linking_between_frames.png" /></a>
    </figure>

      ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- ------------------------------------------

      [[`link_contour_levels`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_contour_levels "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_contour_levels"){.reference .internal}                              Match all contour levels.
      [[`link_frame_size_and_position`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_frame_size_and_position "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_frame_size_and_position"){.reference .internal}   Match frame geometry.
      [[`link_isosurface_values`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_isosurface_values "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_isosurface_values"){.reference .internal}                     Match isosurface values.
      [[`link_slice_positions`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_slice_positions "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_slice_positions"){.reference .internal}                           Match slice positions.
      [[`link_solution_time`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_solution_time "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_solution_time"){.reference .internal}                                 Match current solution time.
      [[`link_value_blanking`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_value_blanking "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_value_blanking"){.reference .internal}                              Match all value blanking style settings.
      [[`link_view`{.xref .py .py-obj .docutils .literal .notranslate}]{.pre}](#tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_view "tecplot.plot.Cartesian3DPlotLinkingBetweenFrames.link_view"){.reference .internal}                                                            Match the view orientation and position.

[[XYLinePlotLinkingBetweenFrames.]{.pre}]{.sig-prename .descclassname}[[group]{.pre}]{.sig-name .descname}[¶](#tecplot.plot.XYLinePlotLinkingBetweenFrames.group "Link to this definition"){.headerlink}

:   Group number (1-32).

    Each frame may be a member of a single group and may opt in or out
    of linking each specific style to other frames within this group.
    Once the group is set, the frame may opt in and out of specific
    attributes:

    :::: {.highlight-python .notranslate}
    ::: highlight
        >>> frame_linking = plot.linking_between_frames
        >>> frame_linking.group = 5
        >>> frame_linking.link_frame_size_and_position = True
        >>> frame_linking.link_solution_time = True
    :::
    ::::

    Type[:]{.colon}

    :   [[`int`{.xref .any .docutils .literal
        .notranslate}]{.pre}](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#int "(in Python v3.13)"){.reference
        .external}
